<result>Wave 7 complete. Report:

## What shipped — Blender-style editor shell

**1. Edit-mode viewport inset (rendering):** `ViewportManager.SetViewportInset(left,top,right,bottom)` / `ClearViewportInset()` / `HasViewportInset`. The aspect-fit `DestinationRectangle` (and pixel-perfect rect) are computed inside the remaining centered sub-rectangle, and `ScaleMouseToVirtualCoordinates` inverts that **same** rect — compositing and mouse mapping can never disagree; margin clicks map to null. Zero inset is byte-identical to the old letterbox (regression-asserted). Resize recomputes automatically.

**2. Native-resolution chrome:** new `RenderTargetID.Editor` (justified vs `Scroll`: Scroll is virtual-coordinate + aspect-fit; Editor is physical-pixel + 1:1), `EditorChromeRenderSystem` (wraps one screen-space `MasterRenderSystem`; native RT recreated on resize; `CurrentTarget` null outside Edit), `RenderLayer.Native(provider)` + optional `TargetProvider` on `RenderLayer` (null ⇒ layer skipped ⇒ chrome invisible and costless in Play — the old per-entity blanking workaround was deleted), `MasterRenderSystem.Destination` now mutable for retargeting. `EditorChromeLayout` (pure) + `EditorChromeBuilder`: opaque dark panels — top bar 44px RGB(32,32,38), right strip 280px RGB(26,26,31) (reserved for Wave-8 panel), bottom strip 24px — and the toolbar rebuilt in real pixels (same 8 actions), labels = PPMondwest 48px BitmapFont at exactly 1/3 ≈ 16px rendered at native res (integer divisor minimizes artifacts; documented limit: still a bitmap downscale). Old `EditorToolbarBuilder` deleted. `ToolbarSystem` hit-tests raw `ScreenPosition` + hover tint.

**3. Consistency:** `EditorShellSystem` syncs inset/relayout/cursor with RunMode each frame (`Dispose` restores — no leak onto the menu). Double-cursor: in Edit the OS cursor shows (`setOsCursorVisible` → `game.IsMouseVisible`) and the game sprite hides; Play reverts. New `CursorInputComponent.OutsideViewport` (default-false polarity keeps all injections valid) mutes margin presses in Selection/Gizmo and margin scroll/pan in CameraNav; `EditorOpReplaySystem` injects it false. Registrar entries: `editor.shell` (update), `editor.renderChrome` (draw). Gizmo/camera-nav otherwise unchanged.

The gizmo, rotation and scale handles aren't clickable when they're outside of the object/entitie's bounds

<task-notification>
<task-id>a7a2a13e708bb47d0</task-id>
<tool-use-id>toolu_01RyK1ggynJaLenUi6w7tDoe</tool-use-id>
<output-file>REDACTED.output</output-file>
<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Fix gizmo handle click-ownership" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Done. Report:

**Confirmed root cause** (the prime suspect, verified in code): `GizmoSystem.ProcessDrag` (update pipeline) begins a handle drag on the `LeftButtonPressed` edge, while `SelectionSystem` — deliberately ordered at the end of the draw pipeline (post-YSort) — processed the **same press edge the same frame**: `SelectionSystem.PreUpdate` armed the pick (old SelectionSystem.cs:97–119), no sprite candidate contained a handle point lying outside the sprite (rotate ring = 40px, scale handle = 48px diagonal from the pivot), and `PostUpdate` (old :152–167) ran `ClearSelection()` → the selection died in the very frame the drag began → next frame `GizmoSystem.Update` (:131–137) found no selection → `CancelDrag()`, overlays/proxies despawned. Two confirmed variants: a handle overlapping *another* sprite re-picked it and `ApplyDragEdit` (which used the current selection, not the drag-start target) retargeted the drag; and the proxy's move handle at the shape's centre (not on the border, often over no sprite) hit the same clear, despawning the family via `ProxySyncSystem`.

**Fix — explicit click-ownership claim (ECS-pure):** new `GizmoStateComponent.PressClaimed` (frame-scoped flag on the existing shared editor-state entity — extended, no new component). `GizmoSystem` writes it **every** Edit frame (true when the press landed on the active tool's handle or a drag is in progress; false otherwise — can't go stale); `SelectionSystem` skips a claimed press entirely (no re-pick, no clear), beside the existing `OutsideViewport` mute. Ordering dependency (update-pipeline write → same-frame draw-pipeline read) is documented in the premise. Drag also now cancels if the selection moves off `_dragTarget` mid-drag (Delete/undo — clicks can't anymore). Preserved: genuine click-empty clears, click-another-sprite re-selects, release never clears, 8b proxy border-pick untouched.

**Adjacent bug found and fixed:** `SelectionSystem` was an `AEntitySetSystem`, whose `Update` early-outs entirely on an empty set — a scene with zero rendered sprites could never border-pick proxies nor click-empty clear. Converted to a plain `ISystem&lt;GameState&gt;` over its own candidate set (no API break; exposed by the new sprite-less proxy test).

**Files changed:** `MonoDreams/level-editor/Component/GizmoStateComponent.cs`, `System/GizmoSystem.cs`, `System/SelectionSystem.cs`, `MonoDreams.Tests/LevelEditor/{GizmoTests,ProxyTests}.cs`, `MonoDreams/level-editor/docs/{premises,overview}.md`, `docs/flows/level-editor.md`.

**Tests (failing-first):** 7 new — 6 `GizmoTests.ClickOwnershipTest_*` + `ProxyTests.REDACTED`; the 5 repro tests were watched failing pre-fix, the 2 behavior guards passed pre-fix.

**Verify:** core build 0 errors → Examples.Desktop build 0 errors → `dotnet test MonoDreams.Tests/` **203/203 green** (196 + 7 new).

**Commits pushed to `feat/level-editor`:** `2d22a1e` (fix + tests), `b3e0ccf` (docs). Ledger updated with the full entry.</result>

**Transport UX as shipped:** under `--editor`/`MONODREAMS_EDITOR=1` (now the ONLY door) the shell/chrome/panel are always composed — no key collapses them. Transport buttons dispatch in both modes; editing buttons (tools/Save/Load/Undo/Redo/Snap) dispatch only while Paused and render with a new dimmed fill while Playing. Selection/gizmo/proxies/camera-nav stay Edit-guarded — a viewport click belongs to the game while Playing; the systems panel stays interactive in both states.

**RunMode decision:** kept `GameState.RunMode` as the axis (Paused=Edit, Playing=Play); nothing in the gate matrix changed — only WHO flips it (new `EditorTransport`, held by the overlay; boot under flag = Paused).

**Restart boundary (new premise):** exclusion by editor markers — every editor-owned entity is tagged `EditorInfrastructureComponent` at creation (chrome, panel rows, gizmo overlays/state, proxies, overlay cursor); the cursor pipeline and screen-`KeepAlive` sub-graphs (ChildOf-transitive; game screen names the dialogue UI root) also survive. Order: Pause → `EditorHistory.Clear()` → remove `CurrentLevelComponent`/`CurrentBackgroundColorComponent` (parsers react to component-ADDED) → sweep-dispose → screen-recorded `Reload` (game screen re-publishes `LoadLevelRequest(levelId)`; menu re-runs its UI builder; runner re-runs its creates). Restart while Playing lands Paused; reloadless Restart is a loud no-op; unsaved edits are discarded (documented loudly in tenet + premise).

**Removed end-to-end:** `EditorModeToggleSystem`, F1/`InputState.Editor`/"Editor" replay action, `EditorInputBindings.ToggleEditRequested`, DefaultEditorKeys F1, menu Edit buttons, `LevelEditorScreen` + `ScreenName.LevelEditor` (desktop + web heads), headless `ToggleMode` op (→ `Play`/`Pause`/`Restart`).

**Autonomous decisions:** dispatch signature widened to `Action&lt;EditorToolbarAction, GameState&gt;`; headless ops are deterministic set-state rather than toggles; `KeepAlive` predicate + ChildOf-descendant propagation added for system-constructed screen infrastructure (dialogue UI).

**Residuals:** Demos screens register no `Transport.Reload` (Restart is a loud no-op there — inline scene builds; noted in ledger); web-head run flag still deferred; disabled-button affordance is fill-only; the pre-existing Blender-level `LevelLoadRequestSystem` error noise also fires on restart (same as first load).</result>

# Schedule Cloud Agents

You are helping the user schedule, update, list, or run **cloud** Claude Code agents. These are NOT local cron jobs — each routine spawns a fully isolated cloud session (CCR) in Anthropic's cloud infrastructure, either on a recurring cron schedule or once at a specific time. The agent runs in a sandboxed environment with its own git checkout, tools, and optional MCP connections.

## First Step

The user has already told you what they want (see User Request at the bottom). Skip the initial question and go directly to the matching workflow.


## What You Can Do

Use the `RemoteTrigger` tool (load it first with `ToolSearch select:RemoteTrigger`; auth is handled in-process — do not use curl):

- `{action: "list"}` — list all routines
- `{action: "get", trigger_id: "..."}` — fetch one routine
- `{action: "create", body: {...}}` — create a routine
- `{action: "update", trigger_id: "...", body: {...}}` — partial update
- `{action: "run", trigger_id: "..."}` — run a routine now

(Note: the API uses `trigger_id` as the parameter name, but the user-facing term is "routine".)

You CANNOT delete routines. If the user asks to delete, direct them to: https://claude.ai/code/routines

## Create body shape

For a recurring schedule:

```json
{
  "name": "AGENT_NAME",
  "cron_expression": "CRON_EXPR",
  "enabled": true,
  "job_config": {
    "ccr": {
      "environment_id": "ENVIRONMENT_ID",
      "session_context": {
        "model": "claude-sonnet-5",
        "sources": [
          {"git_repository": {"url": "https://github.com/roo-oliv/monodreams"}}
        ],
        "allowed_tools": ["Bash", "Read", "Write", "Edit", "Glob", "Grep"]
      },
      "events": [
        {"data": {
          "uuid": "<lowercase v4 uuid>",
          "session_id": "",
          "type": "user",
          "parent_tool_use_id": null,
          "message": {"content": "PROMPT_HERE", "role": "user"}
        }}
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

For a one-time run, replace `"cron_expression": "CRON_EXPR"` with `"run_once_at": "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ"` (RFC3339 UTC, must be in the future). Everything else is identical.

Generate a fresh lowercase UUID for `events[].data.uuid` yourself.

## Environments

Every routine requires an `environment_id` in the job config. This determines where the cloud agent runs. Ask the user which environment to use.

Available environments:
- Ambiente Cloud Padrão (id: env_01TnjMD8mAmdBqnPoArCLXEE, kind: anthropic_cloud)

Use the `id` value as the `environment_id` in `job_config.ccr.environment_id`.


## API Field Reference

### Create Routine — Required Fields
- `name` (string) — A descriptive name
- Exactly ONE of:
  - `cron_expression` (string) — 5-field cron in UTC. **Minimum interval is 1 hour.**
  - `run_once_at` (string) — RFC3339 UTC timestamp. Must be in the future. Fires once, then auto-disables.
- `job_config` (object) — Session configuration (see structure above)

### Create Routine — Optional Fields
- `enabled` (boolean, default: true)
- `mcp_connections` (array) — MCP servers to attach:
  ```json
  [{"connector_uuid": "uuid", "name": "server-name", "url": "https://..."}]
  ```

### Update Routine — Optional Fields
All fields optional (partial update):
- `name`, `cron_expression`, `run_once_at`, `enabled`, `job_config`
- `mcp_connections` — Replace MCP connections
- `clear_mcp_connections` (boolean) — Remove all MCP connections

### Cron Expression Examples

The user's local timezone is **America/Sao_Paulo**. Cron expressions and `run_once_at` timestamps are always in UTC. When the user says a local time, convert it to UTC but confirm with them: "9am America/Sao_Paulo = Xam UTC, so the cron would be `0 X * * 1-5`." For one-time runs, the same conversion applies — "run this at 3pm" → `"run_once_at": "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:00:00Z"` with their 3pm converted to UTC.

- `0 9 * * 1-5` — Every weekday at 9am **UTC**
- `0 */2 * * *` — Every 2 hours
- `0 0 * * *` — Daily at midnight **UTC**
- `30 14 * * 1` — Every Monday at 2:30pm **UTC**
- `0 8 1 * *` — First of every month at 8am **UTC**

Minimum interval is 1 hour. `*/30 * * * *` will be rejected.

### Current Time (for one-off runs)

When /schedule was invoked it was **Fri, Jul 3, 2026 at 12:08 AM** (America/Sao_Paulo) / **2026-07-03T03:08:41.320Z** UTC. Treat this as an approximate anchor only — the conversation may have been running for a while since then.

**Before computing any `run_once_at` value, you MUST re-check the current time** by running `date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ` via the Bash tool. Do not guess or infer today's date from conversation context. Resolve relative requests ("tomorrow at 9am", "in 3 hours", "next Monday") against the freshly fetched time, then echo the resolved local time AND the UTC timestamp back to the user for confirmation before creating the routine. If the resolved time is already in the past, ask the user to clarify rather than silently rolling forward.

## Workflow

### CREATE a new routine:

1. **Understand the goal** — Ask what they want the cloud agent to do. What repo(s)? What task? Remind them that the agent runs in the cloud — it won't have access to their local machine, local files, or local environment variables.
2. **Craft the prompt** — Help them write an effective agent prompt. Good prompts are:
   - Specific about what to do and what success looks like
   - Clear about which files/areas to focus on
   - Explicit about what actions to take (open PRs, commit, just analyze, etc.)
3. **Set the schedule** — Ask when and how often. The user's timezone is America/Sao_Paulo. When they say a time (e.g., "every morning at 9am"), assume they mean their local time and convert to UTC for the cron expression. Always confirm the conversion: "9am America/Sao_Paulo = Xam UTC." If they want a one-time run (e.g., "once at 3pm", "tomorrow morning", "remind me to check X later"), use `run_once_at` instead of `cron_expression` — same timezone conversion applies. **First re-check the current time with `date -u` via Bash** (the reference time above may be stale in a long conversation), resolve the relative phrase against that fresh value, and confirm the resulting absolute timestamp with the user.
4. **Choose the model** — Default to `claude-sonnet-5`. Tell the user which model you're defaulting to and ask if they want a different one.
5. **Validate connections** — Infer what services the agent will need from the user's description. For example, if they say "check Datadog and Slack me errors," the agent needs both Datadog and Slack MCP connectors. Cross-reference with the connectors list above. If any are missing, warn the user and link them to https://claude.ai/customize/connectors to connect first. The default git repo is already set to `https://github.com/roo-oliv/monodreams`. Ask the user if this is the right repo or if they need a different one.
6. **Review and confirm** — Show the full configuration before creating. Let them adjust.
7. **Create it** — Call `RemoteTrigger` with `action: "create"` and show the result. The response includes the routine ID. Always output a link at the end: `https://claude.ai/code/routines/{ROUTINE_ID}`

### UPDATE a routine:

1. List routines first so they can pick one
2. Ask what they want to change
3. Show current vs proposed value
4. Confirm and update

### LIST routines:

1. Fetch and display in a readable format
2. Show: name, schedule (human-readable), enabled/disabled, next run, repo(s)

### RUN NOW:

1. List routines if they haven't specified which one
2. Confirm which routine
3. Execute and confirm

## Important Notes

- These are CLOUD agents — they run in Anthropic's cloud, not on the user's machine. They cannot access local files, local services, or local environment variables.
- Always convert cron to human-readable when displaying
- When listing routines, `ended_reason: "run_once_fired"` means a one-shot already ran (shows as "Ran" in the web UI). The user can re-arm it by updating with a new `run_once_at`.
- Default to `enabled: true` unless user says otherwise
- Accept GitHub URLs in any format (https://github.com/org/repo, org/repo, etc.) and normalize to the full HTTPS URL (without .git suffix)
- The prompt is the most important part — spend time getting it right. The cloud agent starts with zero context, so the prompt must be self-contained.
- To delete a routine, direct users to https://claude.ai/code/routines

<result>All done — committed and pushed. Final report:

## HiDPI findings (instrumented runs on this Mac, before → after)

- **Root cause confirmed with evidence:** MonoGame DesktopGL 3.8.4 creates its SDL window **without `SDL_WINDOW_ALLOW_HIGHDPI`** (verified in the shipped assembly IL: `SdlGameWindow.CreateWindow` passes flags `0x60A`). On this Retina Mac (`backingScaleFactor=2`), the stock run had window client = backbuffer = **1920×1080 logical points**, OS-upscaled 2× — every pixel, including the "native" Wave-7 chrome, was blurred. This was indeed the highest-impact fix.
- **Fix (editor runs only):** new `MonoDreams/level-editor/Composition/EditorHiDpi.cs` re-backs the GL surface at device resolution at runtime from the host side — AppKit via `objc_msgSend` (`contentView.wantsBestResolutionOpenGLSurface = YES` + `NSOpenGLContext.update`), then widens `PresentationParameters` to points × scale **without** `ApplyChanges` (which would physically grow the window). **After: 3840×2160 device px behind the same 1920×1080-point window**, proven by full-frame-coherent 3840×2160 screenshots (impossible without a real re-back). Two empirically-found timing traps: `Window.ClientBounds` is stale (800×480) in `Initialize` → apply on first `Update` + every resize; size read from live `contentView.bounds` (arch-aware NSRect return). Kill switch `MONODREAMS_EDITOR_HIDPI=0`; non-mac/non-Retina/headless/unflagged runs are logged no-ops, byte-identical.

## DPR model (one space)

`ViewportManager.DevicePixelRatio` (default 1) is the single carrier. `CursorInputSystem` multiplies raw (logical) mouse into backbuffer pixels; `EditorChromeLayout`/`SystemsPanelLayout` constants are now logical points × scale; builder/panel scale metrics and label glyphs (`LabelScale × DPR`); shell applies the scaled inset. Invariant tested: **chrome hit-test space == chrome render space at DPR 2**.

## Overlays (gizmos, selection outline, proxy outlines) → native Editor layer

- New pure seams: `Transform/OverlayProjection.cs` (world→virtual via camera matrix→screen via aspect-fit destination; sizes scale by the **fit factor, never zoom** — same apparent size as the old 1/Zoom compensation, rasterized at device res) and `Transform/OverlayMeshClip.cs` (triangle clip to the game viewport rect — nothing draws over letterbox bars; opaque panels cover margins via depth bands: proxy 0.02 &lt; gizmo 0.04 &lt; panels 0.1).
- Emission is a **draw-phase pass** (`System/EditorOverlayPrepSystem.cs`, entry `editor.overlayPrep` after `editor.selection` in all 8 editor-composing screens of both hosts) calling `GizmoSystem.EmitOverlays`/`ProxySyncSystem.EmitOverlays` — gizmo/proxySync run before cameraNav in update order, so emitting there would swim one frame behind pans/zooms. Overlay entities: `Target=Editor`, no `VisibleComponent` (chrome rule). **Hit-testing unchanged** (world/virtual space; click-ownership intact); the scale-handle visual is projected from the exact point `HandleHit` tests. `SelectionSystem` proxy candidates rank at constant `ProxyBorderPickDepth` (decoupled from the visual depth — the raw 0.02 would lose every pick).

## Fonts

Chrome labels: PPMondwest 48px source at `LabelScale × DPR` → 32 device px at DPR 2 — same 16-pt physical size, double density (strictly sharper than 16px OS-upscaled; the 1.5 divisor vs integer 3 is the documented bitmap-font cap; dedicated small-font export = follow-up).

## Verify + evidence

- Core + Examples.Desktop + Demos: 0 errors; web head (`-p:MonoDreamsPlatform=web`): 0 errors; `dotnet test MonoDreams.Tests/` → **228/228 green** (212 + 16 new: `OverlayProjectionTests` incl. hand-computed inset mapping, zoom-invariance of emitted sizes, clipping; `EditorShellTests` DPR suite).
- Visual self-verification: real `--editor` runs with an editor-op plan driving a selection; before/after screenshots (1920×1080 vs 3840×2160), 1:1 crops show crisp gizmo (yellow outline, white move handle, axis arms) and properly-sized sharp DPR-2 toolbar.

## Commits (pushed to `origin feat/level-editor`)

- `c335d10` feat(level-editor): device-resolution editor — HiDPI backbuffer, DPR-aware chrome, native-res overlays
- `1ad3acc` docs(level-editor): codify the native-overlay + device-pixel-ratio invariants (shell/gizmo/toolbar/selection/proxy premises, rendering viewport-inset premise gains `DevicePixelRatio`, flow + overview + recipe updated). Ledger updated.

## Autonomous decisions &amp; residuals

Decisions: HiDPI is editor-scoped (run flag only — game look unchanged, normal runs byte-identical); `EditorHiDpi` lives in level-editor/Composition (shared by both desktop hosts, self-guards via `OperatingSystem.IsMacOS`); emission stayed on the same systems as a public method + 2-line delegator pass (no new component, no ownership transfer); optional `ViewportManager` ctor params (null = identity fit) kept every existing test on the same code path. Residuals: web DPR (canvas `devicePixelRatio`) is a documented follow-up; monitor-swap with different scale needs a resize to re-trigger; `ColliderDebugSystem`/`SpriteDebugSystem` remain virtual-res game diagnostics; ImGui debug inspector (DEBUG builds) would misalign at DPR 2.</result>

Let's take a moment to take a step back and brainstorm a little, a thing that I should've made before implementation. I'm an experienced web backend software developer but not an experienced game developer. I saw a great opportunity to leverage the ability AI have to create custom tailored software at low cost and fast in this coding-first game engine by making the game editor part of the game and allow gamedevs to essentially code their games' features and have the editor treat them as first class citizens and be narrowed to the game's actual needs. At the same time, the flexibility this game engine delivers can break this promise, because we need basics to work reliably, like being able to place new entities, move them, etc. Can you do a blindspot pass to help me figure out my relevant unknown unknowns and help me prompt you better.

---

You're absolutely right to push back on having a game anchoring the engine development and I'm building a game, the editor came from this necessity but then this became a rogue quest on making features up for this editor. So let's reprioritize, scope and reframe the next features based on what I need to accomplish right now:
I'm developing a cozy and playful investigation game. It's 2D top down, similar to Wytchwood/Don't Starve/Cult of the Lamb. The idea is that you can freely walk through an island, talk to NPCs, take pictures of evidence and connect "the dots" on your detective board. The basics like collision, buttons, mouse, camera, already work, but I found myself stuck not being able to assemble an initial level to test these mechanics. The main pain points is that I want to just be able to use some base assets to create a scene that feels real, fully texturized, and I just know how to work with single sprites, but here I need to place bigger sprites like buildings but also create the ground, a road. Also, I need to have a good way to define colliders, shapes, boundaries and positioning. That's the core of what I am missing today. Forget about the next waves, now I need you to think of how to deliver on this and solve these pain points. Take your time to think of ways thoroughly, how each way would leverage this engine's unique concepts and how each way delivers what one would expect from a mature game engine like Unity, Unreal or Godot. The final output should be a .md file for me to review, weigh in and decide how we'll build this. Don't rush it, if there are questions you wanto to make to me before writing the file, that you think will substantially define the architecture or features, ask those and a clear and explanatory way why that's important for me to jump in before you continue.

### Milestone verdict — MET

### Load-bearing autonomous decision
**Baked segments and static footprints are `Passive=true`, not the task's literal `passive=false`.** In this engine `Passive` means "does not initiate a collision" (the `WallEntityFactory`/zone idiom — verified via the DebugInspector legend and empirically: a non-passive segment *drifts* to x=574 when the player pushes it). A passive collider is never moved by resolution yet an active body is resolved out of it — it blocks while staying static. Consequently the blocker-vs-trigger distinction is the game's collision-type classifier keyed on `EntityInfo` identity (the `GameCollisionHelper`/`ZoneDialogueTriggerSystem` pattern), not the `Passive` flag. This is documented in the bake premise.

### Residuals / handoffs for Slice 4
- The editor's `+Box` footprint action is still `Passive=false` (Slice-2 contract, test-asserted); static island props should get passive footprints — add a passive toggle or change the default.
- A reloaded scene's entities are not `SceneObjectComponent`-tagged (`SceneReaderSystem` doesn't re-tag) → Load→edit→Save writes nothing (pre-existing Wave-3 gap surfaced here); have the reader re-tag loaded roots.
- Whole-boundary gizmo-move doesn't re-bake the segments (root-level collision limitation); a thickness handle + whole-move re-bake are Slice-4 comfort items.
- Windowed visual self-verification remains environment-blocked (Cocoa hang); everything verified in-process.

## Slice 3.5 — correctness fixes: DONE

### Root causes confirmed (file:line)
- **BUG 1 (CRITICAL):** `SceneReaderSystem.On` (`MonoDreams/level-editor/System/SceneReaderSystem.cs:90`) reconstructed entities via `SceneSerializer.Deserialize` but never restored `SceneObjectComponent` (transient, deliberately unregistered/never serialized). `SceneWriter` serializes only `[With(SceneObjectComponent)]` roots + their ChildOf closure (`SceneWriter.cs:98`) → a reloaded scene had **0 tagged roots** → the next Save wrote an empty scene, silently losing all work since loading.
- **BUG 2:** `EditorCommandSystem.AddBoxCollider`/`AddConvexCollider` (`EditorCommandSystem.cs:286,305`) called `ColliderComponentCommand.AddBox/AddConvex` at the default `passive=false`. Per the verified Slice-3 semantics (`Passive=true` = "does not initiate a collision" = static blocker), a `Passive=false` static footprint is moved by resolution — the building drifts when walked into. The `AddBox` XML doc stated the semantics **backwards** ("false blocks, true is a sensor") — the conceptual root.

### The fixes
- **Bug 1:** new `SceneReaderSystem.RetagSceneRoots(scene, created)`, called after `Deserialize`, before texture rehydration. Re-tags each **scene root** = every top-level `entities[]` entry (`Parent` null or out of the created range — the exact in-scope test the parent-wiring uses), mirroring `SceneWriter.CollectMembership`'s seed set. **save→load→edit→save is now a fixed point.** ChildOf descendants aren't re-tagged (redundant — already in the ancestor's closure); bake products can't be reintroduced (never serialized → never in `created`, and the writer already skips `BakedProductComponent` subtrees — verified at `SceneWriter.cs:117`). Kept `SceneSerializer.Deserialize` pure because the two undo commands share it and own their own root re-tag.
- **Bug 2:** new `ColliderDefaults.FootprintPassive = true`; the two footprint add-actions now pass it. Corrected the backwards `AddBox`/`AddConvex` docs (passive = static-vs-active, NOT blocker-vs-trigger — that's the game's `EntityInfo` classification; footprints and triggers are both passive). Generic command default stays `false` (matches `BoxColliderComponent`); footprints pass `FootprintPassive` explicitly.

### Files changed
Code: `SceneReaderSystem.cs`, `ColliderDefaults.cs`, `EditorCommandSystem.cs`, `ColliderComponentCommand.cs`. Tests: `SceneRoundTripTests.cs`, `ColliderActionTests.cs`. Docs: `MonoDreams/level-editor/docs/premises.md` (both premises updated with the new invariants + `Tests:` named).

### Tests (failing-first)
- Bug 1: `SceneRoundTripTests.ReloadedSceneReTagsRoots_LoadEditSaveIsAFixedPoint` — save mixed content (prop + boundary with baked children + trigger) → reload → edit a loaded transform → re-save; asserts same 3 roots reproduce, boundary bake child stays excluded, edit persists. (Pre-fix the re-tag count is 0 and the second save is empty.)
- Bug 2: flipped the Slice-2 `Assert.False(box.Passive)`→`Assert.True` with a note; asserted convex footprint is passive; added `AddBoxFootprint_IsPassiveStaticBlocker_BlocksActiveBodyWithoutDrifting` — drives the real collision+physics pipeline in-process: active body blocked, footprint owner doesn't move.

### Verify (exact)
`dotnet build MonoDreams/MonoDreams.csproj` → 0 errors; `MonoDreams.Examples.Desktop` → 0 errors; `MonoDreams.Demos` → 0 errors; `dotnet test MonoDreams.Tests/` → **313 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (311 prior + 2 new). **Milestone `WalkableIslandMilestone` still passes.**

### Commits (pushed to origin feat/level-editor, PR #31; base `19371b3`)
`2ed987a` fix reader re-tag (Bug 1) · `1279335` fix footprint passive default (Bug 2) · `48dacfb` test fixed-point + passive-blocking · `b7230a7` docs premises. Each carries the required trailers.

### Autonomous decisions
(1) Re-tag in `SceneReaderSystem`, not `SceneSerializer.Deserialize` (which the undo commands share and re-tag themselves). (2) Root = `Parent`-null top-level entries only (mirrors the writer; descendant re-tag is redundant). (3) Generic command default kept `false`; footprints pass `FootprintPassive` explicitly — no redundant default flip.

<result>PS1 is complete. All work is committed and pushed to `feat/level-editor`, and the ledger is updated.

## Summary

PS1 (canonical byte-stable serialization + stable scene-local ids) — pure hardening of the existing scene serializer, no change to load semantics, component set, or feature behavior. Only the *bytes* are now deterministic.

### Files
New:
- `MonoDreams/level-editor/Serialization/CanonicalJson.cs` — the single canonical JSON policy
- `MonoDreams/level-editor/Component/SceneEntityIdComponent.cs` — persisted stable scene-local id
- `MonoDreams.Tests/LevelEditor/SceneCanonicalSerializationTests.cs` — 7 determinism tests

Changed: `SceneData.cs` (`id` field), `ComponentSerializerRegistry.cs` (generalized structural-capture set + `MarkStructurallyCaptured&lt;T&gt;()`), `EngineComponentSerializers.cs` (route bodies through `CanonicalJson` + sort `activeLayers` + mark the id component), `SceneWriter.cs` (canonical `Save` + stable-id ordering), `SceneReaderSystem.cs` (canonical deserialize + id restore), `docs/premises.md` + `docs/scene-format.md`.

### Canonical policy chosen
One shared `JsonSerializerOptions`: indented (net8.0 hardcodes LF, verified), `WhenWritingNull`, `[JsonPropertyName]`-respecting, plus a converter emitting every `Dictionary&lt;string,_&gt;` (the `components{}` map) with `StringComparer.Ordinal` keys; `Serialize` appends a trailing `\n`. STJ's default float format is already culture-invariant + shortest-round-trippable (empirically verified under `de-DE`; `1.0`→`1` still round-trips identically — no custom converter added). **All** scene JSON — `SceneWriter.Save`, every component body (`SerializeToElement`), and the reader — flows through it. Collider `activeLayers` (a `HashSet`) is written sorted.

### Stable ids
New `SceneEntityIdComponent` on each serialized root, written as the entry's top-level `id` field (like `parent`, not a component body). Assigned lazily at first serialization (max present + 1), `entities[]` ordered by owning-root id with a **stable** sort (closures stay contiguous), restored on load by `RetagSceneRoots` → preserved across load→save. Marked structurally-captured so it never trips the unregistered-component warning. Deliberately **not** an `EditorIdComponent` overload (that stays the per-session render tiebreak).

### Verify (exact)

## PS3 — editor writes named `.mdscene` into the project SOURCE tree — DONE

## Summary

**PS4 makes the game boot bundled `.mdscene` files as real levels, native-first, via the portable read path — with LDtk/Blender kept as migration fallback.**

### What was built
- **Native-first dispatcher** — `LevelLoadRequestSystem` (level-loading) gained an optional `Func&lt;string,bool&gt; tryLoadNativeScene`, probed *before* the LDtk attempt; on a hit it short-circuits (no `Content.Load`, no `CurrentLevelComponent` removal — the clobber is avoided), else the legacy LDtk/Blender path runs unchanged. The delegate is a plain `Func` so level-loading never depends upward on level-editor. One dispatcher decides native-vs-LDtk.
- **Native-boot bridges** (new, level-editor): `NativeLevelLoader.CreateProbe` probes `Content/Levels/&lt;id&gt;.mdscene` via `TitleContainer` and publishes a `LoadSceneRequest` so the **same** `SceneReaderSystem` serves the game boot (both run modes, no editor composed); `ManifestBoot` reads the bundled `game.mdproj` via `TitleContainer` and boots `startScene` only when its native scene exists.
- **Reader composed in the game screen** — reuses the editor overlay's reader when present, else builds a standalone one, so a shipped game boots native scenes too (inert for the current LDtk/Blender levels).
- **Bundling** — MGCB `/copy:` in `Content.mgcb` (mirrors `game.mdproj`) + a Nopipeline `Content.npl` `Levels/*.mdscene` copy group. **Verified it lands at `bin/.../Content/Levels/sample.mdscene` (desktop) and `wwwroot/Content/Levels/sample.mdscene` (web/KNI)** — TitleContainer-readable everywhere.
- **Sample scene** — committed canonical `Content/Levels/sample.mdscene` (2 engine-component entities), byte-locked by a test.
- **startScene boot** — wired in `Game1`, guarded on native-existence. Manifest kept `startScene="island"` (absent) so the default Examples boot (menu) is unchanged; it auto-flips when island lands in PS5.

### Verify (exact)
- `dotnet build` core / Examples.Desktop / Demos → **0 errors** each.
- **Web head** (`-p:MonoDreamsPlatform=web`, wasm-tools present, content touched) → **0 errors**, sample bundles to `wwwroot/Content/Levels/`.
- `dotnet test MonoDreams.Tests/` → **365 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (351 prior + 14 new). **`IslandMilestoneTests.WalkableIslandMilestone` still passes.**

`16b23c1` dispatcher+bridges · `7569b7e` bundling+sample+boot · `dde8c49` tests · `5beaff5` docs.

### Autonomous decisions of note
- Dispatcher via a `Func` param (not upward message re-publish, not fragile dual-subscriber). Native reader composed in both screen paths (single reader, never double-subscribe). Bundling via MGCB `/copy:` (only all-platform path incl. web wwwroot), **avoiding the guarded csproj entirely** (Content-file edits only). startScene kept as `island` to preserve default UX; the loop is proven by tests + the headless boot test.

## PS6 complete — persistence phase closed

**Bundling mechanism chosen + validated: editor-appends-`/copy:`-line** (banked decision 2's sanctioned fallback). I investigated the *preferred* build-time glob empirically and proved it unworkable here: Nopipeline's `.targets` globs `**/*.npl` relative to the **head** project, but the `.npl` lives in the sibling `Examples.Core` — so Nopipeline finds nothing (explains PS4's "no regen"). Running `npl.dll` on a backup confirmed a full regen *merges* hand-added entries but its recursive `./*.png` group sweeps **828 gitignored Island placeholder PNGs** into the MGCB build (breaks a fresh checkout). A raw-copy `.targets`/`&lt;None&gt;` also can't reach the web `wwwroot/Content/` (only the KNI builder stages there, via the `.mgcb`). So `/copy:` is the one all-platform path, and the editor appends its line on first Save (`MgcbLevelBundle.EnsureCopyEntry`, pure + idempotent; wired in `EditorOverlay`, desktop-only, gated on a resolved project root). **No `.mgcb`/`.npl`/csproj/`.targets` edits** — it's a runtime code path. Validated: sample + Blender_Level land byte-identical in `Examples.Desktop/bin/.../Content/Levels/` **and** `Examples.Web/.../wwwroot/Content/Levels/`.

**Ship-readiness lint** (`SceneLint`, pure): `IsShipReady`/`FindFileAssetKeys` recursively scan serialized component bodies for any `file:`-scheme string (via `FileAssetKey.IsFileKey` — robust to future file-scheme refs). A scene ships-clean iff zero `file:` keys. Surfaced as (a) the pure fn, (b) a test scanning committed `Content/Levels/**` (Blender_Level + sample assert clean), and (c) a loud non-blocking warning on Save.

**Docs consolidated:** 2 new level-editor premises (ship-readiness + zero-touch bundling, both with Tests named); corrected the level-loading bundling premise (it overstated a live "glob … materialized in Content.mgcb" → now the editor-append truth); CORE_TENETS §9 persistence paragraph; scene-format.md (status refresh + Ship-readiness + Bundling sections); overview.md; flow doc Save step; island-authoring-plan Save ref; project-persistence-plan → **IMPLEMENTED** (§3/§10/§11 with the glob→editor-append deviation + Level_0 deferral); roadmap persistence-phase section + deferrals.

**`Tests: none yet` sweep result:** the editor+persistence work added **zero** untested premises (verified via `git diff main...HEAD` across every `premises.md`). The remaining `none yet` entries (level-loading base, camera/collision/cursor/etc.) are pre-existing bootstrap-era premises, unchanged by this phase — out of scope, justified.

- `f455fba` test: both regressions
- `2e08a66` docs: premises

### Verify (exact result)
`dotnet build MonoDreams/MonoDreams.csproj` → 0 errors · `MonoDreams.Examples.Desktop` → 0 errors · `MonoDreams.Demos` → 0 errors · `dotnet test MonoDreams.Tests/` → **393 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (was 387; +6). Milestone `WalkableIslandMilestone` still green. Web head not rebuilt (no platform/content change).

### BUG #2 fix (Save landed in bin)
The walk-up matched the MGCB-copied `bin/.../Content/game.mdproj` first. Now: the walk-up **rejects any `bin`/`obj`-segment candidate**; it detects the repo/solution root (`.git` file-or-dir — worktree-safe — or `*.sln`) and **recursively searches for the source `game.mdproj`, excluding bin/obj**; multiple source manifests resolve deterministically (shallowest path, then ordinal, so `.Core/Content` beats `.Web/wwwroot/Content`). `MONODREAMS_PROJECT_ROOT` stays the trusted primary override; output-copy-only → `Resolved=false`, Save disabled with an actionable message. This makes a normal in-repo run resolve the source **with no env var**.

### BUG #3 fix (reloaded scene blank)
Reloaded sprites had no `DrawComponent` (transient, unserialized), so `SpritePrepSystem`'s `[With(DrawComponent,…)]` query never prepped them. The reader now **restores a sprite `DrawComponent`** (Target mirrors the sprite's own) for every reconstructed sprite, and **auto-frames the camera** on loaded content's AABB when there's no active follow target (leaves it alone when there is).

### Exact Rider setup the user now needs
- Zero-config: an in-repo `dotnet run`/Rider run now resolves `MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Content/game.mdproj` automatically, and Save lands in `MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Content/Levels/&lt;id&gt;.mdscene`.
- To be explicit / when running relocated: set `MONODREAMS_PROJECT_ROOT` in the run config to the content project dir — the absolute path to `MonoDreams.Examples.Core` (which contains `Content/game.mdproj`).

### What the regression tests prove
- Resolution: a bin base-dir layout with source + output + web copies + a `.git` marker resolves the SOURCE, never the bin copy (fails pre-fix); env override wins; output-only → unresolved.
- Render: reloaded sprite HAS a `DrawComponent`; after auto-frame the REAL `CullingSystem` tags the off-origin sprite `VisibleComponent` — i.e. it satisfies both draw-path gates at the content region (a GraphicsDevice-free proof it would paint non-blank). Both fail pre-fix.

### Key autonomous decisions
- Render assertion is an **in-process Culling+DrawComponent+Visible proxy**, NOT `RunDemosAsync`: no Demos screen loads a native `.mdscene` and no test in the repo builds a `GraphicsDevice`, so a windowed/real-paint path isn't a sandbox-safe established pattern. The proxy exercises the exact chain whose break caused the blank screen; only the final GPU blit is unexercised (stub texture). Stated plainly in the ledger.
- Directory probes added as **optional injected delegates** on the pure `Resolve` (defaulted null), not new `IPlatformServices` members — avoids rippling to ~15 fakes + the Web head; the desktop-only `Resolve()` wires `System.IO`.

### One working-tree action to flag
The tree also carried a dirty `MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Content/Content.mgcb` with a dangling `/copy:./Levels/island.mdscene` line (auto-appended by the buggy Save while `island.mdscene` went to bin, not source). It broke the content build for Examples.Desktop **and** `dotnet test` (Tests references Examples.Core). I reverted just that file to HEAD (`git checkout --`) — it's uncommitted bug fallout in the exact machine-generated format, not a deliberate edit, and not one of the two guarded files. It self-heals: the first post-fix Save re-appends the (idempotent) line once the scene lands in source.

**Guarded files confirmed untouched:** `MonoDreams.Examples.Core/MonoDreams.Examples.Core.csproj` and `MonoDreams/camera/Component/CameraFollowTargetComponent.cs` remain modified + uncommitted; they never appear in any FW1 commit diff.

Residual/handoff for the Save/Load dialog wave: resolution is now zero-config in-repo (surface `ProjectContext.LevelsPath` as the confirmed write dir); a multi-scene browser can list `LevelsPath/*.mdscene` and rely on auto-frame so loaded scenes land visible; the shipped non-editor reader is still camera-less (harmless — thread a camera later if a prop-only shipped boot wants framing).</result>

## FW2 — Save &amp; Load dialogs for the in-game level editor

**What I built.** The toolbar Save/Load buttons now open modal dialogs over the real editor pipeline instead of writing/reloading immediately.
- **Save dialog:** a name field prefilled with the current scene id + Save/Cancel. Confirm sanitizes the name to a safe file id (letters/digits/`-`/`_`, edge-trimmed; empty → refused, dialog stays open), sets it as the scene id, and writes `LevelsPath/&lt;name&gt;.mdscene` through the *same* guarded `SaveCurrentScene` (FW1's `SaveBlock` gate still applies — blocked while Playing / unresolved-root; existing `SceneWriter` + ship-lint + zero-touch bundling). Existing target files are overwritten with a logged note (simpler than a confirm sub-dialog).
- **Load dialog:** lists `LevelsPath/*.mdscene`; a row click publishes `LoadSceneRequest(path, fromContent:false)` so FW1's DrawComponent-restore + auto-frame make the scene land centered. Unresolved project → the actionable "set MONODREAMS_PROJECT_ROOT" message, no crash.

**Dialog design (decision).** Editor-native, *not* the `ui` `DialogComponent`/`DialogSystem` — those toggle `VisibleComponent` and trap focus via `UIFocusSystem`, both Main/HUD mechanisms the native-resolution Editor-target chrome must not use (adding `VisibleComponent` breaks the chrome-render invariant). Instead it's built like its sibling `SystemsPanelSystem` (`SimpleButtonComponent` + `DynamicTextComponent` on `RenderTargetID.Editor`, `ScreenPosition` hit-test, DPR-scaled pure layout, shown/hidden by parking off-screen) while reusing the shared UI primitives (no new draw components).

**Text input.** Poll-based keyboard capture (injectable `Func&lt;KeyboardState&gt;` seam) mirroring the repo's `ui.TextInputSystem`/`DefaultEditorKeys` idiom, so the module stays game-agnostic and the field model (`EditorTextField`, append/backspace/set/clear + `Sanitize`) is a pure unit-testable type.

**Modality (two centralized halves, zero edits to the tool systems).** (1) Mouse — the dialog weaves early (`editor.dialog`) and, after hit-testing its own controls, consumes the cursor's pointer edges, so no mouse-driven editor system acts. (2) Keyboard — the screen wires the host keyboard system's existing `AKeyboardInputHandlingSystem.ShouldSuppressInput` to `Dialog.IsOpen`, one hook per host that kills all editor/game keys including Escape-to-exit (critical: typing z/y would otherwise undo/redo). Escape closes the dialog.

**Headless ops (existing grammar intact).** `dialog:save-open | load-open | name &lt;text&gt; | confirm | cancel | load &lt;id&gt;` in `DispatchNamedAction`.

**Files.** New: `MonoDreams/level-editor/System/EditorDialogSystem.cs`, `.../UI/EditorTextField.cs`, `.../UI/EditorDialogLayout.cs`, `MonoDreams.Tests/LevelEditor/EditorDialogTests.cs`. Modified: `.../Composition/EditorOverlay.cs` (mutable `_sceneId`; construct/expose `Dialog`; reroute Save/Load; extract `SaveCurrentScene`/`LoadScene`; `ListScenes` + `dialog:` grammar), `.../docs/premises.md` (new dialog premise + amended save-blocked premise), and 9 screen files (Examples 3 + `DemoEditor` + 5 demo screens) for the `editor.dialog` weave + keyboard-suppress wiring.

**Verify (no Docker, no windowed runs).** `MonoDreams` → 0 errors; `MonoDreams.Examples.Desktop` → 0 errors; `MonoDreams.Demos` → 0 errors; `dotnet test MonoDreams.Tests/` → **409 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (393 prior + 16 new). `IslandMilestoneTests.WalkableIslandMilestone` (milestone) still green.

- `17da6a2` feat(examples): wire the dialogs into every overlay screen
- `16d6ee8` test(level-editor): Save/Load flow, field model, modality

**Working-tree guard.** The two guarded user files (`MonoDreams.Examples.Core/MonoDreams.Examples.Core.csproj`, `MonoDreams/camera/Component/CameraFollowTargetComponent.cs`) remain modified + uncommitted — confirmed untouched (never staged; per-file staging only). No other dirty file encountered (no `Content.mgcb` fallout this time).

**How it works for the user now.** Click **Save** → a dark modal shows the name field (defaulting to the current scene) → type a name / edit with Backspace → **Save** writes `&lt;name&gt;.mdscene` into the source `Content/Levels/` (git sees it), or **Cancel**/Escape aborts. Click **Load** → a modal lists the saved scenes → click a row to load it (it appears centered), or **Cancel**. While either dialog is open, clicks and keys go only to the dialog — the tools behind it don't act.

## REPORT — arm `experiment/editor-inspector-fable`

**Branch:** `experiment/editor-inspector-fable` (single commit on top of `feat/level-editor`), pushed to origin.
**Commit SHA:** `36a70d38ff5ca286bcc20c91dcf57cf9562a8960`

### Verify result (authoritative full gate)
- `dotnet build MonoDreams/MonoDreams.csproj` → 0 errors
- `dotnet build MonoDreams.Examples.Desktop` → 0 errors
- `dotnet build MonoDreams.Demos` → 0 errors
- `dotnet test --configuration Release` → **fully green**: `MonoDreams.Tests` **444 passed** / 0 failed, `MonoDreams.Cli.Tests` **31 passed** / 0 failed. (Baseline was 420+31; net +24 in MonoDreams.Tests after retiring the old 13-test `SystemsPanelTests` and adding 37 new tests.)

### What I built (all four items, engine-native, no ImGui, on the native-res Editor layer, `ScreenPosition`/DPR-correct, registered as the same `editor.systemsPanel` entry so it stays in the systems panel)

I extended the Wave-8 systems panel into **one vertically-scrolling right strip of three collapsible sections** — `EditorPanelSystem` (retiring `SystemsPanelSystem`, keeping the `editor.systemsPanel` entry name + the overlay's `SystemsPanel` hook so all 4 weave sites and self-protection are unchanged).

1. **Collapsible sections + collapsible pipeline groups.** Each section header (Systems/Scene/Inspector) toggles its whole body. Systems **group rows** now carry a disclosure arrow (the documented deferral) that collapses their children; clicking the group **body** still does the Gmail-style enabled cascade — arrow vs. body are distinct hit zones. In-session state lives in the pure-data `EditorPanelStateComponent`.
2. **Entity scene tree** (Scene section). `Inspector/EntitySceneTree.Build` builds a `ChildOfComponent` tree (roots first, children indented, pre-order), hiding `EditorInfrastructureComponent` entities (a child of a hidden parent re-parents to its nearest included ancestor). Rows labelled by `EntityInfoComponent` name→type→stable panel id. **Two-way selection:** a row click sets `SelectedComponent` (the same tag `SelectionSystem` sets from the viewport; panel clicks are `OutsideViewport`, so neither path clobbers the other), and the current selection is highlighted green in the tree. Parent rows collapse their subtree.
3. **Per-entity component list** (Inspector section, bound to the selection). `Inspector/ComponentInspector.Inspect` enumerates a component via DefaultEcs `ReadAllComponents` (an `IComponentReader`, exactly how `ComponentSerializerRegistry` discovers components).
4. **Component member values (read-only).** `ComponentInspector.ReadMembers` reflects public fields + readable non-indexer properties into `name: value` rows — culture-invariant floats, null→`null`, length-capped, and every getter guarded per-member so an arbitrary component (struct/class/tag/ref/null) never throws. Members hidden until you expand a component ("see the state when I want").

**How the tree/inspector reflection works:** the pure `EditorPanelModel.Build` assembles the flat row list from the registrar entries + the tree nodes + the selection's component info + the collapse state (no world, no GraphicsDevice — the crux of the tests). The system does the world I/O and renders through a **pooled** set of row visuals sized to the visible window (entity count is bounded by the window, not the row count — needed because Scene/Inspector content is dynamic). Headless-drivable via new overlay ops: `panel:systems|scene|inspector`, `panel:group &lt;name&gt;`, `panel:inspect &lt;type&gt;`, `panel:select &lt;name&gt;`.

### Files
- **Added:** `Component/EditorPanelStateComponent.cs`, `Inspector/EntitySceneTree.cs`, `Inspector/ComponentInspector.cs`, `UI/EditorPanelModel.cs`, `System/EditorPanelSystem.cs`; tests `EditorPanelTests.cs`, `EditorPanelModelTests.cs`, `EntitySceneTreeTests.cs`, `ComponentInspectorTests.cs`.
- **Changed:** `Composition/EditorOverlay.cs` (constructs the panel, `panel:*` grammar), `UI/SystemsPanelLayout.cs` (arrow gutter/rect, checkbox-less label pos, moved label helpers), `docs/premises.md` (updated systems-panel premise + new "right strip is a stack of collapsible sections" premise with `Tests:` named), `docs/overview.md`.
- **Removed:** `System/SystemsPanelSystem.cs`, `MonoDreams.Tests/LevelEditor/SystemsPanelTests.cs` (behavior re-covered by `EditorPanelTests`).

### Tests added (all required cases covered)
Section collapse + group collapse (`EditorPanelModelTests`, `EditorPanelTests`); entity-tree build with hierarchy + editor-infra hidden + re-parenting (`EntitySceneTreeTests`); tree-row-select ↔ `SelectedComponent` both ways + headless `SelectEntityByName` (`EditorPanelTests`); component list (`ComponentInspectorTests`, `EditorPanelTests`); member-value reflection incl. mixed field types + invariant floats under a comma-decimal culture + nulls + a tag component (`ComponentInspectorTests`); pooling bounds entity count; systems-panel regression (toggle stops the gated system, self-protection, scroll clamp, tri-state).

### Autonomous decisions
- **Unified single-scroll panel over 3 stacked independent systems** — cleaner UX (one scroll), uniform collapse, and a single pure row model; the cost was rewriting `SystemsPanelTests` (behavior preserved, re-tested).
- **Item 3 in the Inspector, not the tree** (the spec allowed either). Scene = pure structure/selection; Inspector = the selection's components (item 3) expandable to members (item 4). No duplicate expansion logic; documented in the premise.
- **State as a class component** (`EditorPanelStateComponent`) on an editor-infra entity to honor "panel/tree state in pure-data components."
- **Row-visual pooling** (label+arrow+checkbox+minus-bar per slot) because Scene/Inspector content is dynamic — bounds entity count to the visible window.
- **ASCII disclosure glyphs** (`&gt;`/`v`) rather than Unicode triangles (BitmapFont glyph coverage isn't guaranteed).
- **Scene filter** = `With&lt;TransformComponent&gt; Without&lt;EditorInfrastructureComponent&gt;` as the "scene entities" proxy.
- Reverted an unrelated `packages.lock.json` (KNI web packages) churn a Release restore introduced.

### Residuals / next
- Long member/label rows aren't truncated to panel width (member values are capped at 80 chars, so overflow is minor + cosmetic). A measure-based truncation (like the palette cards) is the natural follow-up.
- Bake products (`BakedProductComponent` children) show in the tree — the spec only asked to hide editor-infra; hiding regenerated bake products could be a future toggle.
- Value editing is out of scope v1 (read-only display, as specified).
- No full windowed/headless-game integration test for the `panel:*` op grammar (the overlay ctor needs a GraphicsDevice); the underlying public methods and the pure model are unit-tested, and the grammar is wired into `DispatchNamedAction`.</result>

## Report — `experiment/editor-inspector-opus`

**Branch:** `experiment/editor-inspector-opus` (branched from `feat/level-editor` @ `4038626`), pushed to origin. Commits:
- `6e8484c` feat — code + tests
- `2734414` docs — premises + flow

### What I built (all four items)

I evolved the existing single-purpose systems panel (`SystemsPanelSystem`, the `editor.systemsPanel` registrar entry) into the **right-column panel**: one system rendering a vertical stack of three collapsible sections concatenated into one flat wheel-scrolled list. I deliberately kept the class name / registrar entry / `EditorOverlay.SystemsPanel` property so **no screen wiring changed** (documented as a rename-deferral). Collapse/expand + component-expand state lives in the pure-data `EditorPanelStateComponent` (ECS purity).

1. **Collapsible sections + collapsible groups.** Three sections — SYSTEMS / SCENE / INSPECTOR — each header toggles its whole body. Within SYSTEMS, group rows now get a **collapse caret** in a reserved left caret column (`SystemsPanelLayout.CaretRect`); clicking it folds the group's children without touching enable state (the caret region is carved out from the Gmail enable-toggle, so existing center-of-row enable clicks are preserved). This retires the documented Wave-8a deferral.
2. **Entity scene tree** (`SceneTreeBuilder`, pure): roots first, `ChildOfComponent` descendants indented, `EditorInfrastructureComponent` entities hidden (a child of a hidden/dead parent is promoted to root), cycle-safe, labelled by `EntityInfoComponent` name→type→`EditorId`→stable hash. Two-way with selection: a row click sets `SelectedComponent` (single-select, mirroring `SelectionSystem`); the currently-selected entity (picked in viewport or tree) is highlighted (`SelectedLabelColor`). Scrolls with the shared strip scroll.
3. **Per-entity component list** — answered by the INSPECTOR bound to the current `SelectedComponent` (the spec's "your call" option; documented). `ComponentInspector.Inspect` enumerates attached components via DefaultEcs `Entity.ReadAllComponents` (the same mechanism `ComponentSerializerRegistry` uses), sorted by type name.
4. **Component member values (read-only)** — each INSPECTOR component row is collapsible; expanding it shows public fields/properties as "name: value" rows via `ComponentInspector.Members` (reflection). Defensive: handles structs/refs/nulls/throwing getters without throwing (`&lt;error&gt;`/`null`), caps member count + value length.

**How the tree/inspector reflection works:** `SceneTreeBuilder` is pure over `IEnumerable&lt;Entity&gt;` (uses `ChildOfComponent`); `ComponentInspector` boxes each component via an `IComponentReader` then reflects public members with per-getter try/catch and invariant formatting. The panel reads all entities via `world.GetEntities().AsSet()`, rebuilds the dynamic SCENE+INSPECTOR rows only when a cheap signature (entity-set hash / selection / component-set hash) changes — no per-frame chrome churn. Everything is native-resolution Editor-target chrome (no `VisibleComponent`), DPR-scaled, `ScreenPosition`-hit-tested. Headless-drivable via `panel:section|group|component|select:&lt;arg&gt;` in `EditorOverlay.DispatchNamedAction` → panel public methods.

<result>Both fixes are complete, committed, and pushed. Here is the report.

## Summary

### Fix A — Save/Load dialog clicks do nothing (CONFIRMED root cause)
**Root cause (not coordinates, not weave-order):** `EditorDialogSystem.HandleMouseAndConsume` (`MonoDreams/level-editor/System/EditorDialogSystem.cs:282-284`) consumes the cursor's held button **level** state (`input.LeftButton = … = false`) every frame the dialog is open — not just the edges. `CursorInputSystem` (`MonoDreams/cursor/System/CursorInputSystem.cs`, old `var prevLeft = input.LeftButton`) derived its press/release edges by diffing against that same mutable level field. With `LeftButton` forced false every frame, `prevLeft` is always false, so `LeftButtonReleased = !LeftButton &amp;&amp; prevLeft` can never become true. The dialog acts on `LeftButtonReleased` (cs:267) → structurally unobservable while open → clicks dead. The toolbar that *opens* the dialog worked because nothing consumes the level state until the dialog is open. The woven order (`input.cursor` entry 1 → `editor.dialog` entry ~4, both UPDATE phase) is correct — the defect is self-inflicted edge poisoning.

**Fix (general, framework-level):** added `PreviousLeftButton/Right/Middle` to `CursorInputComponent`; `CursorInputSystem` now derives edges from those (written straight from the hardware read each frame, before any consumer runs), mirroring how `ScrollWheelDelta` derives from the un-consumed `ScrollWheelValue` accumulator. Any pointer-edge consumer — present or future — is now safe; the dialog keeps its full consume (strongest downstream inertness). Byte-identical for every non-consuming screen. Added a `MouseStateProvider` seam so the test drives the real pipeline.

**Failing-first test proves it:** `EditorDialogTests.SaveDialog_ClickThroughRealCursorPipeline_ConfirmsOnRelease` drives a scripted press→release through the **real** `CursorInputSystem → editor.dialog` `SequentialSystem`. I temporarily reverted the edge logic and watched it FAIL (`savedId` null — release never fires), then restored and it passes. This exercises the CursorInputSystem→consume→CursorInputSystem loop that the injected-edge tests (`SkipHardwareRead`) always bypassed.

### Fix B — editor window resizable
`Window.AllowUserResizing = true` on the editor run path in both desktop heads (`MonoDreams.Examples.Desktop/Game1.cs`, `MonoDreams.Demos/Game1.cs`), gated `_editor &amp;&amp; !headless` (shipped/non-editor + 1×1 headless unchanged). The resize path already existed but was unreachable: `OnWindowResize → ApplyEditorHiDpi` (`EditorHiDpi.TryEnable` re-reads contentView bounds × scale each call) `→ InitializeRenderer` (ViewportManager ScreenWidth/Height + DPR + camera matrices); `EditorShellSystem` relayouts chrome + re-applies the DPR-scaled inset; `EditorChromeRenderSystem` recreates the native Editor target; palette/panel relayout on dim change; mouse mapping follows via the single ViewportManager inset. New test `EditorShellTests.ShellSystem_ResizeWithDprChange_RecomputesChromeInsetAndMouseMapping` covers a resize+2×-DPR recompute.

### Files changed
- `MonoDreams/cursor/Component/CursorInputComponent.cs`, `MonoDreams/cursor/System/CursorInputSystem.cs`
- `MonoDreams.Examples.Desktop/Game1.cs`, `MonoDreams.Demos/Game1.cs`
- `MonoDreams.Tests/LevelEditor/EditorDialogTests.cs`, `MonoDreams.Tests/LevelEditor/EditorShellTests.cs`
- `MonoDreams/cursor/docs/premises.md` (new premise), `MonoDreams/level-editor/docs/premises.md` (updated dialog premise + Tests)

## EF2 complete — file-system navigator + triangle arrows

### (A) Save/Load → Blender-style file-system navigator
The toolbar Save/Load buttons now open one shared directory browser (native Editor-target chrome, DPR-scaled, resize-aware):
- A **breadcrumb** of the current path (`Content / Levels / props`), an **Up** button, and a scrollable list of **subfolders** (rendered `name/`, accent-tinted, click to descend) and **`.mdscene` files** (click).
- **Load:** clicking a file publishes the load with the resolved absolute path. **Save:** a filename field + Save button write `&lt;current-dir&gt;/&lt;name&gt;.mdscene` through the existing guarded path; clicking a file fills the field to overwrite. Escape cancels; modal (consumes the cursor via the EF1 release-edge path).
- **Scoping decision:** rooted (up-bounded) at the **project root** so it never escapes into the OS filesystem, and **opens at `LevelsPath` (`Content/Levels`)** — because a scene must live there to be MGCB-bundled + loaded native-first by the shipped game. Only a save written directly into `LevelsPath` is auto-bundled (the `./Levels/&lt;id&gt;.mdscene` copy line can only address that dir); elsewhere it's authored but logged as not-bundled. Unresolved root → the actionable "set `MONODREAMS_PROJECT_ROOT`" message.
- Headless grammar extended: `dialog:cd &lt;folder&gt;` | `dialog:up` | `dialog:pick &lt;file&gt;`, keeping `save-open/load-open/name/confirm/cancel`.

### (B) Triangle disclosure arrows (font-independent)
Every collapsible right-strip row's ASCII `v`/`&gt;` caret is now a **filled-triangle mesh** (▸ collapsed, ▾ expanded) via `FilledTriangleMeshGenerator` + the pure `SystemsPanelLayout.ArrowTriangle`, baked into a raw `DrawComponent` on the Editor target (identity matrix, no `VisibleComponent`) — the gizmo-overlay pattern, removing the BitmapFont-glyph-coverage dependency. Caret hit-zone unchanged, so the enable-toggle vs collapse distinction still holds.

- New: `MonoDreams/level-editor/UI/EditorFileBrowser.cs`, `MonoDreams.Tests/LevelEditor/EditorFileBrowserTests.cs`
- Modified: `MonoDreams/level-editor/UI/EditorDialogLayout.cs`, `.../System/EditorDialogSystem.cs`, `.../Composition/EditorOverlay.cs`, `.../UI/SystemsPanelLayout.cs`, `.../System/EditorPanelSystem.cs`, `.../docs/premises.md`, `MonoDreams.Tests/LevelEditor/EditorDialogTests.cs`, `.../EditorPanelTests.cs`

Let's take a moment to focus on the editor UI and UX itself, it's too rough, too raw.
 1. What we aim is an editor UI & UX just like Blender's UI & UX, we won't have it from day 1, but we need to have a clear path and start marking the terrain, what I mean is, we need to properly structure the editor window, have panes that can be rearranged, capable of scrooling and acomodating any new tools and menus we think of (navigation needs to work also beyond just scrooling, just like Blender we better have tabs to organize our different tools). Basic UX like knowing what's selected, hovering buttons, hovering list itens, being able to use different colors but still stick to a strict color pallete. The visual north for MonoDreams are, surprisingly maybe, interfaces like Claude Code with clear actions that one can take and coloring for intent and bits of animation here and there, minimal, but intentional.
 2. The whole project idea needs a little more intention and be a more consice experience: first, we need a concept for the whole project, so when we launch the editor in the level selection screen, this is part of the project already, should be editable in any way possible, and we should have a clear representation of these screens somewhere in the editor, probably a scenes side panel. So when we save a project, we're saving all scenes configuration files. The editor should allow for loading a different scene that isn't tied to any particular game screen yes, but maybe that doesn't have to be in this early version of the editor. The basics is that we create game screens on code and need a clear way to indicate from which configuration files they load from, then in the editor we'll be editing those. So we don't need a "Load" action infact, just selecting a screen is enough to load the scene tied to it. Saving should actually display a dialog with options to save the scene, save all scenes/the whole project, or saving a backup scene that will be dangling and just ask the user for a scene name, so we can scrap all that file navigator thing right now. Note that when saving a backup scene to a different file, this should save and actually reload the current scene to the original scene file, since that's what tied to on code and we just "threw away" the current edits to a different file that isn't tied to a scene yet.
