# MonoDreams editor plumbing — map for the screen-bound-scenes redesign

All paths repo-relative to the worktree root. I read the four premise docs first; invariants are cited where they bind the design.

---

## 1. SCREENS — registration, creation, switching, editor composition

**Registry mechanism** — `MonoDreams/foundation/Screen/ScreenController.cs`:
- Screens live in a `private readonly Dictionary&lt;string, Func&lt;IGameScreen&gt;&gt; _screenCreators` (line 44). Keys are strings; values are lazy creators.
- `RegisterScreen(string screenName, Func&lt;IGameScreen&gt; creator)` (lines 46-52) — throws on a duplicate name.
- `LoadScreen(string screenName)` (lines 74-84) looks up the creator and sets `_screen.next = creator()` (does not switch yet; throws if unregistered).
- `Update` (lines 54-67) performs the deferred swap: if `_screen.next != null` it calls `_screen.current?.Dispose()`, promotes `next→current`, then `_screen.current.Load(this, Content)`. **The switch disposes the outgoing screen unconditionally — no save/dirty hook.**
- `IGameScreen` (`MonoDreams/foundation/Screen/GameScreen.cs` lines 9-15): `World`, `UpdateSystem`, `DrawSystem`, `Load(...)`.

**Is there an enumerable registry of string keys?** Keys exist but `_screenCreators` is private with **no enumeration accessor** (only `RegisterScreen`/`LoadScreen`). A Scenes panel would need a new public accessor (e.g. expose the registered names, or a richer registration that carries scene metadata). The string keys themselves are declared as constants: `MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Screens/ScreenName.cs` (`Game`, `LevelSelection`, `InfiniteRunner`) and `MonoDreams.Demos/Screens/DemoScreens.cs` (`demos.launcher`, `.camera`, `.physics`, `.dialogue`, `.ui`).

**One world reused, or new world per screen?** **New `World` per screen** — every screen constructs `_world = new World()` in its ctor (LevelSelectionScreen.cs:102, InfiniteRunnerScreen.cs:102, LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:125, all demo screens). Screen `Dispose()` calls `_world.Dispose()` (e.g. LevelSelectionScreen.cs:470). So a screen switch is a **full world teardown + rebuild**. The `GameState` is the *one* exception: a single `_state = new GameState(...)` lives on the controller (ScreenController.cs:31) and **persists across switches**; `RunMode` therefore survives a switch.

**Editor composition per screen under the flag** — the `EditorOverlay` is built inside each screen's `CreateUpdateSystem` when `editorEnabled` (LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:228-286, LevelSelectionScreen.cs:303-322, InfiniteRunnerScreen.cs:326), then `_editor.BindPipelines(...)` + `EditorOverlay.LogComposition(...)` in the ctor tail (LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:131-135). The overlay is built over the screen's own world/camera/layers (`EditorOverlay` ctor, `MonoDreams/level-editor/Composition/EditorOverlay.cs:117-282`). The host boots Paused by mutating the shared state **once** in `Game1.Initialize`: `_screenController.State.RunMode = EditorRunFlag.InitialRunMode(true)` (`MonoDreams.Examples.Desktop/Game1.cs:207`, Demos `Game1.cs:149`); `EditorRunFlag.InitialRunMode` returns `Edit` when enabled (`MonoDreams/level-editor/Composition/EditorRunFlag.cs:58`).

**Editor infra on a screen switch vs. Restart.** The `EditorInfrastructureComponent` restart boundary is **intra-screen only** — it is consumed exclusively by `EditorTransport.Restart` (see §7). On a screen *switch* the whole world is disposed, so editor-infra entities die with it and the new screen constructs a fresh overlay + fresh infra entities; `RunMode` (Edit) persists but nothing re-applies it. Each screen re-registers `Transport.Reload`/`Transport.KeepAlive` in its `Load` (see §2, §7).

---

## 2. EXISTING SCREENS — how each picks its level/scene

**The channel today is a `Game.Services` singleton + a message, not a per-screen binding.**

- **`LevelSelectionScreen`** (`MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Screens/LevelSelectionScreen.cs`): buttons are built with **hardcoded** level strings — `CreateButtonEntity("Level 1", …, "Blender_Level", …)` (line 178) and `CreateButtonEntity("Runner", …, null, …, ScreenName.InfiniteRunner)` (line 179). The level string is stored on a `LevelSelector` component (`Component/UI/LevelSelector.cs`, field `LevelName`/`TargetScreen`).
- **`ButtonInteractionSystem`** (`MonoDreams.Examples.Core/System/UI/ButtonInteractionSystem.cs:69-74`): on click publishes `ScreenTransitionRequest(targetScreen, levelSelector.LevelName)` (targetScreen defaults to `ScreenName.Game`).
- **`LevelSelectionScreen.OnScreenTransitionRequest`** (lines 148-158): stores `Game.Services.AddService(new RequestedLevelComponent(levelIdentifier))` then `LoadScreen(request.ScreenName)`.
- **`LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.Load`** (`MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Screens/LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:165-180`): reads `RequestedLevelComponent` from `Game.Services`, `_world.Publish(new LoadLevelRequest(levelId))` (line 170), records `Transport.Reload = () =&gt; _world.Publish(new LoadLevelRequest(levelId))` (line 176), then **removes the service** (line 179).
- **`InfiniteRunnerScreen`**: **no scene file** — `Load` calls `CreateTreadmill/CreatePlayer/CreateSpawnPoint/CreateScoreHUD` (lines 132-135) and `Transport.Reload` re-runs those create methods (lines 141-147).
- **Demos** (`DemoLauncherScreen` + `camera/physics/dialogue/ui` demo screens): pure code-built content, no level id, no `LoadLevelRequest`. `DemoLauncherScreen` just calls `LoadScreen(DemoScreens.X)` on button click.
- **Boot-time level selection** bypasses the menu: `Game1.Initialize` (lines 227-256) sets `RequestedLevelComponent` from a replay plan's `StartLevel`, or from `ManifestBoot.ResolveStartScene(...)`, then `LoadScreen(ScreenName.Game)`; else it loads `ScreenName.LevelSelection`.

**Natural seam for "my scene file is X".** Three candidate seams already carry this responsibility:
1. `RequestedLevelComponent` (`MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Component/RequestedLevelComponent.cs`) — the current cross-screen "which level" channel (a `Game.Services` singleton).
2. `ScreenController.RegisterScreen(name, creator)` — the registration site could be extended to carry a bound scene id (this is where a Scenes-panel enumeration would read from).
3. `EditorTransport.Reload` (set in each screen's `Load`) — the screen *already* records "how to reload my content"; a screen-bound scene is the declarative form of exactly this callback.
4. `GameProject.StartScene` — the manifest already declares one entry scene (§8), the closest existing "screen/game → scene" declaration.

---

## 3. SCENE PIPELINE — writer/reader, eligibility, components, canonical bytes

**Writer:** `MonoDreams/level-editor/Serialization/SceneWriter.cs`. **Serializer:** `SceneSerializer.cs`. **Registry:** `ComponentSerializerRegistry.cs`. **Reader:** `System/SceneReaderSystem.cs`. **In-memory shape:** `SceneData.cs`.

**Eligibility policy (which entities get written):**
- `SceneWriter.CollectMembership` (lines 149-172): the write set = every `With&lt;SceneObjectComponent&gt;` root **plus** each root's transitive `ChildOfComponent` descendant closure (`AddWithDescendants`, lines 237-257).
- `BakedProductComponent` entities are excluded **even inside a tagged root's closure** (line 249) — boundary segment colliders regenerate on load.
- `SceneObjectComponent` (`Component/SceneObjectComponent.cs`) is a pure tag; **who adds it** is decisive:
  - `Undo/CreateEntityCommand.cs:44,53` (palette placement + undo restore),
  - `Undo/DeleteEntityCommand.cs:61` (undo restore),
  - `Composition/LevelImporter.cs:69` (import op tags parsed content roots),
  - `System/SceneReaderSystem.cs:193` (**re-tag on load** — `RetagSceneRoots`).
  - **No screen code ever tags an entity.** So code-spawned entities — `LevelSelectionScreen`'s buttons/text/layout, the runner's treadmill/player, cursor, all editor chrome/gizmo/panel entities — are **untagged and excluded**. A native-loaded scene's roots ARE save-roots only because `SceneReaderSystem` re-tags them (SceneReaderSystem.cs:185-197). This re-tag is the "`save→load→edit→save` is a fixed point" invariant (level-editor premise "Scene round-trip reconstructs from registered components").

**Which components serialize** — `Serialization/EngineComponentSerializers.cs:64-87` registers: `TransformComponent` (core.Transform), `SpriteInfoComponent` (core.SpriteInfo — `AssetKey` never the live `Texture2D`; SOURCE sort fields `LayerDepth`/`YSortOffset`/`YSortDepthBias`, never derived `DrawComponent.LayerDepth`), `EntityInfoComponent`, `BoxColliderComponent`, `ConvexColliderComponent`, `RigidBodyComponent`, `VelocityComponent`, `CameraFollowTargetComponent`, `BoundaryComponent`; `ChildOfComponent` registered as **structural parent link** (captured as `SceneEntityData.Parent`, lines 80-81); `SceneEntityIdComponent` **structurally captured** as `SceneEntityData.Id` (line 86). Deliberately **not** registered (re-derived): `VisibleComponent`, `ColliderTagComponent`, `DrawComponent`, cursor state (registry doc lines 44-47). Game serializers (`PlayerState`, `OrbitalMotion`, `StopMotionEffect`, `DialogueZoneComponent`) come from `MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Serialization/GameComponentSerializers.cs`, registered on both editor and shipped-reader registries (LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:285, :469).

**Opt-in enforcement** (`ComponentSerializerRegistry.cs`): unregistered component on write → skip + `Logger.Warning` (`ComponentDiscoverer.OnRead`, lines 133-151); unregistered key on read → **throws** (aborts the load, `DeserializeEntity` lines 111-123).

**Canonical byte-stability** (`Serialization/CanonicalJson.cs`, policy summarized in the premise "Scene serialization is canonical and byte-stable"): 2-space/LF indent, invariant floats, null fields omitted, trailing `\n`, `Dictionary&lt;string,_&gt;` keys emitted in `StringComparer.Ordinal` order; set-valued fields sorted (`EngineComponentSerializers.SortedLayers`, lines 395-400). Determinism also needs **stable per-entity ids**: `SceneEntityIdComponent` assigned lazily at first serialization (`SceneWriter.AssignStableIds`, lines 225-235 = max present + 1), preserved across load→save, and `entities[]` is stably sorted by owning-root id (`CollectOrderedMembership`, lines 187-205).

---

## 4. SAVE PATH and LOAD PATH end-to-end

**SAVE (after the EF2 file-browser rework)** — `MonoDreams/level-editor/Composition/EditorOverlay.cs`:
1. Toolbar `Save` → `DispatchToolbarAction` Save case (lines 520-541): checks `SaveBlock(state, _projectContext)` (lines 762-767 — `Playing` first, then `NoProjectRoot`); if blocked, logs and returns; else **opens the dialog** `Dialog.OpenSave(_sceneId)` (line 540) — Save no longer writes immediately.
2. Dialog confirm callback (ctor wiring, lines 249-254): `onSaveConfirmed: (path, s) =&gt; { _sceneId = Path.GetFileNameWithoutExtension(path); SaveCurrentSceneTo(path, s); }`.
3. `SaveCurrentSceneTo` (lines 578-619): re-checks `SaveBlock` (defense-in-depth), logs overwrite note, `var scene = new SceneWriter(Serializer).BuildScene(_world, _camera, _layers)`, `WarnIfNotShipReady(scene)` (lint for `file:` keys), `writer.Save(scene, target)` → `IPlatformServices.WriteAllText` (`SceneWriter.Save`, lines 120-140), then `EnsureLevelBundled()` if the target is directly under `LevelsPath` (`IsUnderLevelsRoot`, lines 623-630).
4. Path resolution: `SceneFilePath(ctx, sceneId)` = `LevelsPath/&lt;sceneId&gt;.mdscene` (lines 792-798); `LevelsPath` = `ProjectRoot + LevelsDir` (`EditorProjectContext.LevelsPath`, EditorProjectContext.cs:105-106). `ProjectRoot` resolved once by `EditorProjectContext.Resolve` (EditorProjectContext.cs:178-246: env `MONODREAMS_PROJECT_ROOT` → walk-up rejecting `bin`/`obj` → repo-root recursive search → Unresolved), created in `Game1.Initialize` (Game1.cs:189) and passed to every screen's overlay.

**Bundling — how it works exactly.** `EditorOverlay.EnsureLevelBundled` (lines 733-754): reads `ProjectRoot/Content.mgcb` via `IPlatformServices.Current.ReadAllText`, calls `MgcbLevelBundle.EnsureCopyEntry(text, sceneId, out changed)` (pure text transform, `Serialization/MgcbLevelBundle.cs:58-73` — appends a `#begin ./Levels/&lt;id&gt;.mdscene` + `/copy:./Levels/&lt;id&gt;.mdscene` block, **idempotent** via whole-line match), and writes back with `IPlatformServices.Current.WriteAllText` (line 750). **So bundling programmatically EDITS `Content.mgcb` by appending a `/copy:` directive** — it is *not* a csproj `&lt;None&gt;` copy and *not* a programmatic MGCB API; it is a raw text append to the `.mgcb` file. (I did not open the forbidden `Content.mgcb`; this is from `MgcbLevelBundle.cs` and `EditorOverlay.cs`.)

**LOAD (native-first, `TitleContainer`)** — two entry points, one reader:
- **Editor Load:** toolbar `Load` → `DispatchToolbarAction` (lines 542-546) → `Dialog.OpenLoad()`; a file pick → `onLoadSelected` → `LoadScene(path)` (lines 638-649) → `_world.Publish(new LoadSceneRequest(path, fromContent:false))`. `SceneReaderSystem.On` (SceneReaderSystem.cs:102-139) reads via `IPlatformServices.ReadAllText` when `fromContent:false`, or `TitleContainer.OpenStream(Content/…)` when `fromContent:true` (`ReadSceneJson`, lines 147-158), deserializes, `RetagSceneRoots`, `RehydrateTextures`, `RestoreDrawComponents`, `AutoFrameLoadedContent`.
- **Game boot:** `LoadLevelRequest(id)` → `LevelLoadRequestSystem.On` (`MonoDreams/level-loading/System/Level/LevelLoadRequestSystem.cs:72-97`) calls the native probe first. `NativeLevelLoader.CreateProbe` (`MonoDreams/level-editor/Composition/NativeLevelLoader.cs:60-78`) checks `Content/Levels/&lt;id&gt;.mdscene` via `TitleContainer` and on a hit publishes `LoadSceneRequest(rel, fromContent:true)` → the *same* `SceneReaderSystem`. No native scene at boot (`enableLegacyLdtkFallback:false`) → **fails loud** (LevelLoadRequestSystem.cs:88-97).

---

## 5. SCENE IDENTITY and DIRTY tracking

**Current-scene tracking:** the editor holds the scene **id** (not a path) in a mutable field `EditorOverlay._sceneId` (EditorOverlay.cs:83), exposed as `SceneId` (line 305). It is initialized in the ctor via `ResolveSceneId(sceneId, projectContext)` (line 142; lines 779-784: explicit id → `manifest.StartScene` → `DefaultSceneId="untitled"`). It is **mutated** by the dialog confirm/pick callbacks (`_sceneId = Path.GetFileNameWithoutExtension(path)`, lines 251-252). There is no stored absolute path — only the derived `SceneFilePath` (lines 792-798). `EditorProjectContext` holds no current-scene field; it holds only project root/manifest/levels dir.

**Dirty flag:** **none exists.** Grep for `dirty/unsaved/isModified/savePoint` in `MonoDreams/level-editor` returns only doc-comment prose ("unsaved edits discarded") — no state. Unsaved edits are simply discarded by Restart/screen-switch.

**Cheapest correct place for a dirty flag:** `EditorHistory` (`MonoDreams/level-editor/Undo/EditorHistory.cs`). **Every** world mutation during editing flows through `History.Push` / transactions — the mutating systems are `System/EditorCommandSystem.cs` (delete/collider/ordering), `System/GizmoSystem.cs` (transform drags), `System/PalettePlacementSystem.cs` (placement), `System/BoundaryToolSystem.cs`, and the proxy/collider commands under `Undo/`. A monotonic edit counter or a "save-point sequence number" on `EditorHistory` (captured at Save, compared to current) gives a correct dirty signal for free, and `EditorHistory.Clear()` (used by Restart) is the natural reset point. (Note: `History.Count == 0` is *not* a correct clean signal — you can undo past a save.)

---

## 6. UNDO — bounded stack, what clears it

`MonoDreams/level-editor/Undo/EditorHistory.cs`: bounded `LinkedList&lt;IEditorCommand&gt;` at `DefaultCapacity = 100` (line 28); push past cap evicts oldest FIFO (`AddEntry`, lines 148-154). `Undo`/`Redo` empty-stack no-ops (lines 116-133). Drag-coalescing via `BeginTransaction`/`CommitTransaction`/`CancelTransaction` (lines 78-113). **`Clear()`** (lines 141-146) drops undo+redo+open transaction **without** applying/reverting.

**What clears it:** only `EditorTransport.Restart` calls `_history.Clear()` (`Composition/EditorTransport.cs:119`). **Scene load does NOT clear it** — `SceneReaderSystem` never touches history, so an editor `Load` (or the native boot load) leaves the previous undo entries intact (they may reference now-disposed entities — a latent hazard, see RISKS). There is exactly one `EditorHistory` per overlay (constructed EditorOverlay.cs:149).

---

## 7. RELOAD — can a scene be re-loaded from disk at runtime?

Yes — the transport **Restart** path is the reload mechanism. `EditorTransport.Restart` (`Composition/EditorTransport.cs:107-131`), in order:
1. `state.RunMode = RunMode.Edit` (line 118),
2. `_history.Clear()` (line 119),
3. `_world.Remove&lt;CurrentLevelComponent&gt;()` + `Remove&lt;CurrentBackgroundColorComponent&gt;()` (lines 123-124 — LDtk-parser re-trigger hygiene),
4. `DisposeSceneEntities()` (lines 133-143) — disposes everything except `Survives(entity)` (lines 146-150: `EditorInfrastructureComponent` OR `CursorControllerComponent`/`CursorInputComponent` OR screen `KeepAlive` predicate, keeps propagating down `ChildOf`),
5. `Reload()` (line 127) — the screen-recorded callback.

**Does it re-issue LoadLevelRequest / does it despawn scene entities first?** Yes to both: it **despawns scene entities first** (step 4), then the game screen's `Reload` **re-publishes `LoadLevelRequest(levelId)`** (LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:176) → native probe → `LoadSceneRequest(rel, fromContent:true)` → `SceneReaderSystem`. A reloadless Restart is a **loud no-op** (EditorTransport.cs:109-116). This is the "reload from original bound file, discard in-memory edits" primitive backup-save needs — **but note the source mismatch:** the game screen's `Reload` reloads the *bundled content copy* via `TitleContainer` (`fromContent:true`), whereas the editor's `Load` reads the *source tree* file via `IPlatformServices` (`fromContent:false`). Backup-reload almost certainly wants the **source** file (what's on disk after discarding edits), not the possibly-stale bundled copy — this is a decision the design must pin. `SceneReaderSystem.AutoFrameLoadedContent` (lines 272-294) will also re-frame the camera on reload when there's no active follow target.

---

## 8. game.mdproj — schema, read/write sites, does it list scenes, LevelsPath

**Schema** (`MonoDreams/level-editor/Serialization/GameProject.cs`), four fields only:
- `formatVersion` (int, default 1) — line 44
- `startScene` (string, default "") — line 49 — a single scene-local id
- `levelsDir` (string, default "Levels") — line 54
- `assetRoots` (string[], default empty, authored order preserved) — line 60
- Constants: `FileName = "game.mdproj"` (line 36), `DefaultLevelsDir = "Levels"` (line 40).

**Committed manifest** — `MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Content/game.mdproj`: `{ formatVersion:1, startScene:"island", levelsDir:"Levels", assetRoots:["Island","Atlas","Objects"] }`.

**Read sites:**
- Editor: `EditorProjectContext.Build` → `CanonicalJson.Deserialize&lt;GameProject&gt;(readAllText(manifestPath))` (EditorProjectContext.cs:314-336), from the **source** tree.
- Game boot: `ManifestBoot.TryReadManifest` → `TitleContainer` read of `&lt;contentRoot&gt;/game.mdproj` (`Composition/ManifestBoot.cs:38-56`), then `ManifestBoot.ResolveStartScene` (lines 65-78) returns `startScene` only if a native `.mdscene` exists for it. Wired in `Game1.Initialize` (Game1.cs:240-252).

**Write sites:** none at runtime — nothing writes `game.mdproj` in the shipped/editor flow (the editor writes only `.mdscene` files and `Content.mgcb`). `CanonicalJson` round-trips it (covered by `GameProjectTests`).

**Does it list scenes/levels today?** **No.** It declares a single `startScene` and a `levelsDir`; there is no scene list and no screen↔scene binding. The class doc explicitly flags this as future: "(later) to list levels for an open/switch UI" (GameProject.cs:15). **LevelsPath** is not in the manifest — it's derived: `EditorProjectContext.LevelsPath = Path.Combine(ProjectRoot, LevelsDir)` (EditorProjectContext.cs:105-106), where `LevelsDir` comes from `manifest.LevelsDir` (falling back to `DefaultLevelsDir`, EditorProjectContext.cs:325).

## 10. PREMISES to update under the new model

**`MonoDreams/level-editor/docs/premises.md`** (quoting titles):
- **"The editor's Save/Load dialog is a modal file-system navigator, project-root-scoped, that owns input while open"** — the load-navigator is deleted; Save becomes a 3-action dialog. Largest rewrite.
- **"The editor toolbar's buttons drive the same shared editor instances; the chrome is native-resolution on the Editor target, always on while the editor is composed"** — Load button removed/repurposed; Save opens the 3-action dialog; a Backup action added.
- **"Save is blocked while Playing or when no project root is resolved"** — extend for Save Scene / Save Project / Save Backup semantics and their per-action guards.
- **"The editor Save writes versioned `.mdscene` into the project source tree"** — still true for Save Scene / Save Project; add the **dangling-file** backup write (a write NOT into the versioned levels dir).
- **"The transport's Restart rebuilds the scene from the original load request and discards unsaved edits"** — backup-save reuses this reload; document the "reload from bound source file" variant.
- **"The editor run flag composes the always-on editor and the transport owns RunMode"** — screens now declare a bound scene; a Scenes panel selection triggers a screen switch/load.
- **"The editor right strip is a stack of collapsible sections: Systems, a Scene tree, and a selection-bound Inspector"** — add the **Scenes** panel (screen list) as a new section/panel.
- **"The project manifest anchors the editor's project root; unresolved is fail-safe"** — manifest likely gains a scene list / screen→scene bindings (today only `startScene`).
- **"Scene round-trip reconstructs from registered components, not factories"** and **"The game boots native scenes native-first via `LoadLevelRequest`"** — the screen-declares-scene binding sits on top of this load path.

**`MonoDreams/level-loading/docs/premises.md`:**
- **"`LevelLoadRequestSystem` resolves `LoadLevelRequest` native-only (fails loud otherwise)"** — screen-bound scene id feeds this dispatcher.
- **"Native `.mdscene` levels are bundled by an MGCB `/copy:` entry and read via `TitleContainer`"** — backup writes go to a dangling file, *not* bundled; document the distinction.

**`MonoDreams/foundation/docs/premises.md`:**
- **"Default `RunMode = Play` preserves all existing pipelines"** — references the boot-Paused editor opt-in and the transport as sole RunMode owner; note that screen switches preserve `RunMode` (the shared `GameState`).

---

## RISKS / SURPRISES

1. **Screen switch silently destroys unsaved edits.** `ScreenController.Update` (ScreenController.cs:58) calls `_screen.current?.Dispose()` → `_world.Dispose()` with **no dirty check and no editor hook**. Selecting a different screen in the Scenes panel = `LoadScreen` = full world teardown = **silent loss of in-memory edits**. There is no interception point today; the redesign must add a dirty gate around `LoadScreen`.

2. **No dirty tracking exists at all** (§5). Backup-save's "discard in-memory edits" and any "unsaved changes?" prompt need a new dirty signal; the only correct home is a save-point marker on `EditorHistory` (undo depth alone is wrong).

3. **Saving on a code-spawned screen writes an EMPTY scene → data-loss footgun.** No screen tags its entities `SceneObjectComponent` (§3); only native-loaded (re-tagged), palette-placed, or editor-created entities are save-roots. On `LevelSelectionScreen`/`InfiniteRunnerScreen` (and demos) the world has **zero save-roots**. If a screen "declares its scene" and the designer hits **Save Scene** (or **Save Project / all scenes**) on such a screen, the writer produces an empty `entities[]` and **overwrites the bound `.mdscene` with nothing**. "Save Project (all scenes)" is especially dangerous: it must not blanket-write every declared scene from whatever's currently in memory.

4. **Two screens can bind the same scene — and already almost do.** Each overlay independently resolves `SceneId` via `ResolveSceneId` → `manifest.StartScene` (EditorOverlay.cs:142,779-784). Every screen whose overlay falls back to the manifest defaults to `startScene="island"`, so `LevelSelectionScreen`, `LoadLevelExampleGameScreen`, and `InfiniteRunnerScreen` **all currently resolve `SceneId="island"`** and would Save to the same `island.mdscene`. `RegisterScreen` dedupes screen *names* (ScreenController.cs:49) but nothing dedupes scene *bindings*. The new model needs explicit per-screen scene ids and collision detection.

5. **Reload source mismatch (bundled vs. source).** Restart's game-screen `Reload` re-reads the **bundled** `Content/Levels/&lt;id&gt;.mdscene` via `TitleContainer` (`fromContent:true`), while editor `Load` reads the **source-tree** file via `IPlatformServices` (`fromContent:false`). Backup-reload ("reload from original bound file") must choose deliberately — the source file is what discards in-memory edits back to disk truth; the bundled copy can be stale until a rebuild. (§4, §7)

6. **Stale undo entries after a load.** Scene `Load` does **not** clear `EditorHistory` (§6) — only Restart does. After an editor Load, undo entries reference disposed pre-load entities (`Undo` would revert against a dangling `Entity`). The new model's screen-select→load must clear history like Restart does.

7. **Restart needs a screen-registered `Reload` or it's a loud no-op** (EditorTransport.cs:109-116). Backup-reload built on Restart will silently do nothing on any screen that forgot to set `Transport.Reload` (demos set none today; only the three Examples screens do). Set in `Load` each time.

8. **`RequestedLevelComponent` is a fragile `Game.Services` singleton.** It's set by the menu (LevelSelectionScreen.cs:153) and *manually removed* by the consumer (LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:179). A screen that doesn't consume/remove it leaks the level id into the next screen. A declarative screen→scene binding should replace this hand-off rather than layer on it.

9. **Camera auto-frames on every load** (`SceneReaderSystem.AutoFrameLoadedContent`, SceneReaderSystem.cs:272-294) when no active `CameraFollowTargetComponent`. A backup-reload will jump/zoom the camera to the content AABB, discarding the designer's pan/zoom — surprising mid-edit.

10. **`CurrentLevelComponent` removal in Restart is LDtk-coupled** (EditorTransport.cs:123-124). For a pure-native screen-bound scene it's a harmless no-op, but the reload sequence still carries LDtk-era hygiene; worth confirming it stays correct when the only load path is native.

11. **`RunMode` persists across screen switches but nothing re-asserts Edit.** The boot-Paused mutation happens once (Game1.cs:207). A screen switch keeps `RunMode=Edit` via the shared `GameState` — correct today, but if any future path constructs a fresh `GameState` per screen this invariant breaks (the editor would boot the new screen in Play).

12. **Web/console screens can't save** (`projectContext == null` → `SaveBlock.NoProjectRoot`, EditorOverlay.cs:762-767; Game1.cs:189 resolves it only on desktop under the flag). A screen-bound-scene model that assumes a writable binding must degrade gracefully where the project context is unresolved (Save disabled loudly, already the pattern).</result>

# Level-Editor Chrome — Full Map (substrate for the Blender-shell refactor)

Repo root: `/Users/rodrigooliveira/.warp/worktrees/monodreams/feat/level-editor`. All `file:line` refs below are repo-relative to that root.

## Invariants that gate this refactor (from the four docs)
- **Editor = the real game under a run flag**, gated by `GatedSystem`/`EditTimeBehavior`, not a forked renderer (level-editor premises; `docs/CORE_TENETS.md`). Chrome renders on `RenderTargetID.Editor` at **native window resolution**, composited 1:1 by `RenderLayer.Native`, above the game layers.
- **Device pixels are the shell's one space.** `ViewportManager.ScreenWidth/Height`, all chrome layout/hit rects, and `CursorInputComponent.ScreenPosition` are backbuffer device pixels; every metric is authored in logical points and multiplied by `DevicePixelRatio` (rendering + level-editor premises).
- **Three render targets, two behaviors**: `Editor` is screen-space, never culled, never aspect-fit; **content authored in virtual coords renders at the wrong scale**. **Chrome entities must carry NO `VisibleComponent`** (it would pull mesh chrome into `MeshPrepSystem`, overwriting the identity `WorldMatrix` their absolute-pixel vertices need).
- **Mesh fills must be opaque** (premultiplied-alpha rule) — a "subtle" translucent panel reads near-white.
- **`DrawComponent.Type` is mutually exclusive** (Sprite/Text/NinePatch/Mesh); one visual type per entity.

---

## 1. INVENTORY (files under `MonoDreams/level-editor/` that render or lay out chrome)

**Pure models / layout helpers (world-free, GraphicsDevice-free, unit-testable):**
- `UI/EditorChromeLayout.cs` — the shell's outer layout math: `TopBar`/`RightPanel`/`BottomBar` rects, `ViewportInset` 4-tuple, `ButtonRow`, `Px(points,scale)`. Fixed 3-region model (top/right/bottom, left=0).
- `UI/SystemsPanelLayout.cs` — right-strip row math: `LineRect`, `ArrowRect`, `CheckboxRect`, `MinusBarRect`, `LabelPosition`, `ArrowTriangle` (disclosure-triangle geometry), scroll math, `ParkedPosition`.
- `UI/PaletteLayout.cs` — bottom-shelf card-grid math: `CardFlow`, `TryCardRect`, `CardIconRect`/`CardLabelRect`/`CardChipRect`, `ThumbnailFit`, `BandRow`, row-scroll math.
- `UI/EditorDialogLayout.cs` — Save/Load dialog rects: `Panel`, `Title`, `Breadcrumb`, `UpButton`, `Field`, `ConfirmButton`/`CancelButton`, list `Row`s.
- `UI/EditorPanelModel.cs` — the **pure right-strip assembler**: `PanelRow` (data), `PanelRowKind`/`EditorPanelSection` enums, `EditorPanelModel.Build(...)` → flat `List&lt;PanelRow&gt;` across Systems/Scene/Inspector.
- `UI/EditorTextField.cs` — pure single-line text-field model (append/backspace/set/`Sanitize`).
- `UI/EditorFileBrowser.cs` — pure filesystem-navigation model (`RawDirectory`/`BrowserRoots` records, `Enter`/`Up`/`Refresh`/`FilePath`/`Breadcrumb`, `.mdscene` filter, project-root-bounded).
- `Inspector/ComponentInspector.cs` — reflection over an entity's components → `name: value` rows (pure logic; reads one entity).
- `Inspector/EntitySceneTree.cs` — pure pre-order tree builder from a candidate pool + `ChildOfComponent`.
- `UI/EditorChromeBuilder.cs` — builder (not a system) that creates the 3 panels + toolbar buttons; **the de-facto central color/depth palette lives here** (see §3).

**ECS systems (view + interaction):**
- `System/EditorChromeRenderSystem.cs` — the chrome render pass: a screen-space `MasterRenderSystem` (null camera) over `Editor` into a native-res target, recreated on resize. `ISystem`.
- `System/EditorShellSystem.cs` — per-frame: applies `ViewportManager.SetViewportInset`, relayouts chrome on window/DPR change, forces the OS cursor. `ISystem`.
- `System/ToolbarSystem.cs` — top-bar buttons: hit-tests `ScreenPosition` vs `ToolbarButtonComponent.Bounds`, hover tint, Play/Pause label sync, click dispatch. `AEntitySetSystem`.
- `System/EditorPanelSystem.cs` — the right strip (Systems/Scene/Inspector): builds rows, hit-tests, pools row visuals, draws disclosure arrows + checkboxes, binds selection. `ISystem`.
- `System/PalettePlacementSystem.cs` — bottom-shelf asset cards + band selector + ghost placement + trigger cards. `ISystem`.
- `System/EditorDialogSystem.cs` — the modal Save/Load browser (keyboard + mouse + modal consume + list rows). `ISystem`.
- `System/EditorCommandSystem.cs` — not a chrome renderer, but the **dispatch target** for the toolbar's selection-edit actions (ordering/collider/vertex). `ISystem`.

**Pure-data components backing chrome:**
- `Component/ToolbarButtonComponent.cs` — action + `Bounds` (device px) + `IsHovered`/`IsActive`.
- `Component/EditorPanelStateComponent.cs` — collapse/expand state for the right strip.
- `Component/EditorToolbarAction.cs` — the toolbar action enum + `IsTransport()`.
- `Component/EditorInfrastructureComponent.cs` — the restart-survival tag (every chrome entity sets it).
- `Component/GizmoStateComponent.cs` — tool/mode/snap (toolbar mutates it; `EditorToolMode` gates viewport-press ownership).

**Composition seam (not a system):** `Composition/EditorOverlay.cs` — constructs every editor system, builds chrome, and owns the two dispatch surfaces `DispatchToolbarAction` (enum) and `DispatchNamedAction` (string ops).

**Mesh primitives used by chrome:** `../rendering/Draw/IMeshGenerator.cs:455` `FilledTriangleMeshGenerator` (disclosure arrows); `../ui/ButtonMeshPrepSystem.cs` (panel/button rects); `../ui/SimpleButtonComponent.cs` (the fill+outline component).

---

## 2. DRAW PATH

**`RenderTargetID.Editor` flow.** `EditorChromeRenderSystem` builds one `MasterRenderSystem(spriteBatch, gd, world, source=Editor, destination=nativeTarget, camera=null)` — null camera = screen-space (`System/EditorChromeRenderSystem.cs:48-51`). The target is recreated to `ScreenWidth×ScreenHeight` whenever the window changes (`:73-82`). It exposes `CurrentTarget` (null when the pass is disabled), which `RenderLayer.Native(() =&gt; ChromeRender.CurrentTarget)` composites 1:1 over the whole window (`../rendering/System/Draw/FinalDrawSystem.cs:32-36`; `Composition/EditorOverlay.cs:240`). **Draw-pipeline order** (`MonoDreams.Examples.Core/Screens/LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:660-672`): `editor.selection` → `editor.overlayPrep` → `renderMain`/`renderUI`/`renderHUD` → `editor.renderChrome` → `finalDraw`.

**DrawComponent element types used by chrome:**
- **Mesh** — panels + buttons are fill-only/outline `SimpleButtonComponent` meshes baked by `ButtonMeshPrepSystem` at absolute pixel positions with `WorldMatrix=Identity` (`../ui/ButtonMeshPrepSystem.cs:35-104`); disclosure arrows are raw `DrawComponent{Type=Mesh}` (`System/EditorPanelSystem.cs:500-522`).
- **Text** — labels are `DynamicTextComponent` on `Editor` (`UI/EditorChromeBuilder.cs:245-255`).
- **Sprite** — palette thumbnails are `DrawComponent{Type=Sprite, Target=Editor}` populated directly (`System/PalettePlacementSystem.cs:866-878`, `:1078-1100`).

**Font + HiDPI scaling.** Labels use the screen's `BitmapFont` (PPMondwest, 48px source) at `LabelScale = 1f/3f` (`UI/EditorChromeBuilder.cs:49`), and every `DynamicTextComponent.Scale` is set to `LabelScale * scale` where `scale = ViewportManager.DevicePixelRatio` (`UI/EditorChromeBuilder.cs:190,202-206`; `System/EditorShellSystem.cs:74`). Text renders through `MasterRenderSystem`'s **LinearClamp** batch (`../rendering/System/Draw/MasterRenderSystem.cs:77`), meshes/sprites through **PointClamp** (`:76`).

**FilledTriangleMeshGenerator / ArrowTriangle.** `FilledTriangleMeshGenerator(a,b,c,color).Generate()` → 3 `VertexPositionColor`, indices `{0,1,2}`, `TriangleList` (`../rendering/Draw/IMeshGenerator.cs:455-479`). `SystemsPanelLayout.ArrowTriangle(arrow, expanded)` computes the 3 points inside the arrow square with an 18% inset — right-pointing ▸ collapsed, down ▾ expanded (`UI/SystemsPanelLayout.cs:145-167`). Drawn as a screen-baked mesh (not a font glyph) so it has zero dependency on the font's Unicode coverage (`System/EditorPanelSystem.cs:414-424`).

**CLIPPING / SCISSOR — NONE in the render stack (critical).**
- `MasterRenderSystem.BeginSpriteBatch` uses `RasterizerState.CullNone` (`../rendering/System/Draw/MasterRenderSystem.cs:114-124`); `ResetGraphicsStateForMeshRendering` also `CullNone` (`:128-135`). No `ScissorRectangle`, no `ScissorTestEnable`, no custom `RasterizerState` anywhere. `FinalDrawSystem` is `CullNone` too (`../rendering/System/Draw/FinalDrawSystem.cs:140-145,156-161`).
- `GraphicsDevice.Viewport` is deliberately **not** set (`../rendering/Renderer/ViewportManager.cs:273-275`).
- The only `scissor` token in the codebase is a doc comment (`../ui/ScrollViewComponent.cs:10`).
- A **CPU triangle clipper exists** — `Transform/OverlayMeshClip.cs` (Sutherland–Hodgman, per-triangle, used to clip gizmo overlay meshes to the game viewport) — but it clips **mesh geometry only**; it cannot clip text (`DrawString`) or sprite thumbnails.

**Conclusion for the shell:** partial-row pixel clipping is **not cheap** today. Options: (a) **row-granular culling** — park whole rows off-screen — which is already the model in all three scrollable regions (`SystemsPanelLayout.VisibleLineCount` shows only fully-visible lines "a partially scrolled line would bleed over the top/bottom bars, which share the Editor target", `UI/SystemsPanelLayout.cs:12-15`); (b) add a scissor-enabled `RasterizerState` + set `GraphicsDevice.ScissorRectangle` inside `MasterRenderSystem` — but that system is the "sole render implementation" and game-agnostic, so it's a cross-cutting change affecting every pass; (c) route a region through `RenderTargetID.Scroll` + `RenderLayer.Overlay` (rendering premise) — but that target is **HUD-virtual space, one region per screen**, not the native `Editor` target. **Row-granular culling is the pragmatic v1.**

---

- `TopBarColor (32,32,38)` `:53`, `SidePanelColor (26,26,31)` `:54`, `BottomBarColor (32,32,38)` `:55`, `ButtonFill (52,52,62)` `:56`, `ButtonHoverFill (76,76,94)` `:57`, `ButtonDisabledFill (38,38,44)` `:60`, `ButtonOutline (150,150,162)` `:61`, `LabelColor (235,235,240)` `:62`, `CheckboxOnFill (96,168,112)` `:63`, `CheckboxMixedMark (26,26,31)` `:67`, `HeaderLabelColor (150,150,162)` `:68`, `DisabledLabelColor (140,140,150)` `:69`.
- Depths: `PanelDepth 0.1` `:73`, `ButtonDepth 0.5` `:74`, `CheckboxMarkDepth 0.55` `:76`, `LabelDepth 0.6` `:77`. `LabelScale 1/3` `:49`.

**Colors defined OUTSIDE that file (not centralized):**
- `System/EditorDialogSystem.cs:76-84` — `BackdropColor (10,10,14)`, `PanelColor (30,30,36)`; dialog depths `BackdropDepth 0.70`, `PanelDepth 0.74`, `ControlDepth 0.80`, `LabelDepth 0.86` (reuses `EditorChromeBuilder` colors for its buttons/labels).
- `System/PalettePlacementSystem.cs:84-96` — `GhostColor = White*0.55`, `ArmedItemFill = CheckboxOnFill`, `ThumbnailDepth 0.56`, `ChipDepth 0.58`.
- `System/EditorPanelSystem.cs:462-475` — `RowColor()` hardcodes `Color.White` for hover, `CheckboxOnFill` for selected rows (otherwise `EditorChromeBuilder` colors).

**Sizes / margins / paddings:**
- `UI/EditorChromeLayout.cs:29-56` — `TopBarHeight 44`, `RightPanelWidth 280`, `BottomBarHeight 168`, `LeftPanelWidth 0`, `ButtonHeight 30`, `ButtonGap 8`, `ButtonPaddingX 10`, `RowMarginX 10`.
- `UI/SystemsPanelLayout.cs:26-59` — `RowHeight 22`, `PaddingX 10`, `PaddingY 8`, `CheckboxSize 12`, `CheckboxGap 8`, `IndentPerDepth 14`, `ArrowGutter 14`, `MinusBarWidth 8`, `MinusBarHeight 2`, `LinesPerNotch 3`.
- `UI/PaletteLayout.cs:27-84` — `HeaderHeight 24`, band `ButtonHeight 20`, `CardWidth 92`, `CardHeight 104`, `CardPadding 6`, `CardIconHeight 66`, `CardLabelGap 4`, `CardLabelHeight 18`, `CardChipWidth 22`, `CardChipHeight 15`, `CardGapX 8`, `CardGapY 8`, `RowsPerNotch 1`.
- `UI/EditorDialogLayout.cs:20-51` — `PanelWidth 520`, `LoadPanelHeight 420`, `SavePanelHeight 470`, `Padding 18`, `TitleHeight 26`, `BreadcrumbHeight 26`, `UpButtonWidth 52`, `FieldHeight 34`, `ButtonHeight 30`, `ButtonWidth 96`, `ButtonGap 12`, `RowHeight 26`.
- Line thicknesses: buttons `1.5f` (`EditorChromeBuilder.cs:269`), panels `0f` (`:234`), checkbox `1.5f` (`EditorPanelSystem.cs:484`), palette buttons `1f` (`PalettePlacementSystem.cs:850`).

**Central style/theme/palette TYPE: none.** The `EditorChromeBuilder` static fields are the closest thing. There is a `../ui/ButtonTheme.cs` (`ButtonVisualSystem`) for game UI, but **chrome does not use it** (chrome buttons have no `ButtonStateComponent`).

## 4. INPUT

**Coordinate space = raw `ScreenPosition` in backbuffer DEVICE pixels.** `CursorInputSystem` sets `ScreenPosition = mouse.Position * DevicePixelRatio` (`../cursor/System/CursorInputSystem.cs:60`). Every chrome hit-test takes `new Point((int)input.ScreenPosition.X, .Y)` and tests it against layout rects that are themselves DPR-scaled — so hit space == render space:
- Toolbar: `System/ToolbarSystem.cs:81` (`_cursorPoint = input.ScreenPosition`), `:100` (`button.Bounds.Contains`).
- Right strip: `System/EditorPanelSystem.cs:216-218,231-233`.
- Palette: `System/PalettePlacementSystem.cs:517-518`.
- Dialog: `System/EditorDialogSystem.cs:305-306`.
(`VirtualPosition`/`WorldPosition` are frozen over the margins — `../cursor/Component/CursorInputComponent.cs:11-19` — so chrome must use `ScreenPosition`.)

**Per-frame HOVER — yes, everywhere, but INSTANT (no fade):**
- Toolbar: `System/ToolbarSystem.cs:100-112` — `over = active &amp;&amp; Bounds.Contains`; instant fill swap between `ButtonFill`/`ButtonHoverFill`/`ButtonDisabledFill`.
- Right strip: `System/EditorPanelSystem.cs:394-407` (`HoveredRow`) → `RowColor(row, hovered)` at `:462-475`, hover = `Color.White`.
- Palette: `System/PalettePlacementSystem.cs:476,496,1120-1164` (`HandleInteraction` returns hovered index, `ReflectState` swaps fill instantly).

**Mouse wheel / scroll delta — yes, exposed and consumed.** `CursorInputComponent.ScrollWheelValue` + `ScrollWheelDelta` (`../cursor/Component/CursorInputComponent.cs:41-42`), derived in `../cursor/System/CursorInputSystem.cs:96-98`. Consumers: right strip `System/EditorPanelSystem.cs:220-222`; palette `System/PalettePlacementSystem.cs:520-522`; dialog list `System/EditorDialogSystem.cs:311-312`; camera-nav zoom (premise). Wheel→lines/rows: `SystemsPanelLayout.ScrollLines` (`/120 * 3`, `UI/SystemsPanelLayout.cs:83`), `PaletteLayout.ScrollRows` (`/120 * 1`, `UI/PaletteLayout.cs:177`).

**Modal input consumption (dialog swallowing clicks).** `EditorDialogSystem.HandleMouseAndConsume` hit-tests its own controls **on the release edge** (`System/EditorDialogSystem.cs:314-332`), then **clears every pointer edge + level + `ScrollWheelDelta` on the single cursor entity** and `NotifyChanged` (`:335-339`). It is woven **early** (`editor.dialog`, before the tools/toolbar — `LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:513`; `EditorOverlay.cs:388-394`), so downstream mouse systems see no edges this frame. Keyboard half: the screen wires the host keyboard's `ShouldSuppressInput = Dialog.IsOpen` (`LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:198`).

**The EF1 fix that makes this work.** `CursorInputSystem` derives press/release edges from `PreviousLeftButton/…` — the *hardware* levels it owns, written straight from the raw read before any consumer runs (`../cursor/System/CursorInputSystem.cs:65-93`; fields at `../cursor/Component/CursorInputComponent.cs:32-40`), **not** from the mutable `LeftButton` level fields. So the dialog clearing `LeftButton` to suppress the click does **not** poison next frame's edge derivation; the dialog itself acts on `LeftButtonReleased` and still sees its own click (documented at `CursorInputSystem.cs:65-71`). Note two more assumptions: a single cursor entity (all hit-test loops `return` after the first — `ToolbarSystem.cs:84`, `EditorDialogSystem.cs:340`), and the dialog swallows the current keyboard state on open so no stale key edge fires (`EditorDialogSystem.cs:166,177`).

## 5. RIGHT STRIP (`EditorPanelSystem` / `EditorPanelModel` / `SystemsPanelLayout`)

**Model↔view split:**
- **Pure model** `UI/EditorPanelModel.cs`: `EditorPanelModel.Build(state, update, draw, sceneNodes, sceneLabel, selected, inspector, inspectorTitle)` → flat `List&lt;PanelRow&gt;` (`:132-163`). Three sections: `Systems` (`AppendPipeline` over `registrar.Entries`, honoring `CollapsedGroups`, `:175-215`), `Scene` (`AppendScene` over `EntitySceneTree` nodes with per-subtree collapse, `:217-252`), `Inspector` (`AppendInspector` over `ComponentInspector` data, `:254-294`). `PanelRow`/`PanelRowKind`/`EditorPanelSection` at `:11-106`.
- **Pure layout** `UI/SystemsPanelLayout.cs`: `LineRect`, `ArrowRect`, `CheckboxRect`, `MinusBarRect`, `LabelPosition[NoCheckbox]`, `ArrowTriangle`, scroll math — all DPR-scaled.
- **State** `Component/EditorPanelStateComponent.cs`: section-collapsed bools, `CollapsedGroups` (by entry name), `CollapsedTreeEntities` (by `Entity`), `ExpandedInspectorComponents` (by full type name), `InspectorEntity`.
- **View/interaction** `System/EditorPanelSystem.cs`: `Update` (`:125-144`) = `SyncInspectorSelection` → `BuildRows` → `HandleInteraction` → `BuildRows` again (so a click's effect shows the same frame) → `ClampScroll` → `PositionVisuals`.

**Rows generated:** `BuildRows` (`:158-175`) materializes the scene set (`With&lt;TransformComponent&gt; Without&lt;EditorInfrastructureComponent&gt;`, `:115-116` — hides editor machinery), builds the tree (`EntitySceneTree.Build`), inspects the selection (`ComponentInspector.Inspect`), and calls `EditorPanelModel.Build`.

**Clicks→rows:** `HandleInteraction` (`:212-238`) — `if (!panel.Contains(point)) return`; wheel→scroll; on `LeftButtonReleased`, loop rows, `vi = i - _scroll`, `LineRect(panel, vi).Contains(point)` → `HandleClick` (`:240-262`): `onArrow = row.Collapsible &amp;&amp; ArrowRect(line, depth).Contains(point)`; switch on `Kind`: `SectionHeader`→`ToggleSection`; `PipelineEntry`→ arrow? `ToggleGroupCollapsed` : `TogglePipelineEnabled`; `SceneEntity`→ arrow? `ToggleTreeEntity` : `SelectEntity`; `InspectorComponent`→`ToggleInspectorComponentKey`.

**Collapsible sections + triangle arrows:** arrows are `FilledTriangleMeshGenerator` screen-baked meshes set/cleared per frame (`:414-424`, `SetArrowMesh`/`ClearArrowMesh` `:512-531`); geometry from `SystemsPanelLayout.ArrowTriangle`. Checkbox = a `SimpleButtonComponent` box + optional minus bar (`:427-452`); tri-state via `PipelineEnabledState` (`Composition/EditorPipelineRegistrar.cs:19-29`, group state derived from descendant leaves).

**Pooled visuals:** `RowVisual{Label, Checkbox, MinusBar, Arrow}` (`:93-99`); `EnsurePool` sizes to the visible line count (`:477-489`); `PositionVisuals` repurposes the pool each frame and parks unused rows off-screen (`:371-392`). Row entities carry `EditorInfrastructureComponent` and **no** `VisibleComponent`.

**Scene tree + inspector bind selection:** `SelectEntity` (`:282-296`) is single-select mirroring `SelectionSystem`; the selected scene row highlights (`RowColor` → `CheckboxOnFill`, `:470`). `SyncInspectorSelection` (`:148-156`) clears `ExpandedInspectorComponents` + updates `InspectorEntity` when the selection changes; the Inspector binds to `FirstSelected()`. Two-way is safe because chrome clicks are `OutsideViewport`, so `SelectionSystem` never clobbers a tree selection. **Self-protection:** `TogglePipelineEnabled` refuses to disable the panel's own entry or any ancestor group (`ContainsPanel`, `:264-280`). Headless surface: `ToggleSection`/`ToggleGroupCollapsed`/`ToggleTreeEntity`/`ToggleInspectorComponentKey`/`SelectEntityByName` (`:300-367`).

---

## 6. BOTTOM SHELF (`PaletteLayout` / `PalettePlacementSystem`)

**Card layout:** fixed cards **92×104 pt** (`UI/PaletteLayout.cs:47-48`) = top icon box (`CardIconHeight 66`) + bottom label row (`CardLabelHeight 18`) + a band-chip badge (`22×15`, top-right of the icon). `CardFlow` packs fixed-width cards left-to-right into columns of the content width, wrapping into rows (`:144-156`). A header row holds the layer-band selector buttons (`HeaderHeight 24`, `BandRow` `:123-137`).

**Band marks:** two kinds — (1) the **global band selector** in the header (`SelectedBand`, highlighted `System/PalettePlacementSystem.cs:1122-1130`); (2) the **per-asset band chip** on each card — a one-letter mark (band initial, or `-` for auto) whose click **cycles** the asset's permanent band (`CycleAssetBand`, `:286-303`), filled with the accent when marked (`:1145-1150`). Resolution rule `ResolveBand` = marked band else global (`:229-236`).

**Scroll / paginate:** it **scrolls by whole card rows** on the wheel (`PaletteLayout.ScrollRows` `:177`, `VisibleRowCount` `:162-166`, `TryCardRect` returns false for scrolled-out rows → parked off-screen `:185-199`; `System/PalettePlacementSystem.cs:520-522,955-968`). **No pagination.** Same row-granular culling as the systems panel; `BottomBarHeight` was raised to 168 to fit one full card row under the header (`UI/EditorChromeLayout.cs:41`).

---

## 7. DIALOGS

**Pieces:** `System/EditorDialogSystem.cs` (modal Save/Load browser) + `UI/EditorDialogLayout.cs` (pure rects) + `UI/EditorTextField.cs` (pure text model) + `UI/EditorFileBrowser.cs` (pure fs-nav model, injected `listDirectory`).

**Modality mechanism:** `IsOpen = Mode != None` (`:112`). Mouse = clear-all-edges consume (see §4, `:335-339`). Keyboard = screen wires `ShouldSuppressInput = Dialog.IsOpen`. Closed = all entities parked off-screen (`ParkAll`, `:571-580`); no `VisibleComponent`.

**Focus / typing model:** a single implicit focus = the Save name field (no per-field focus objects). Save mode reads the keyboard directly each frame (`ReadSaveKeyboard`, `:272-288`): poll `GetPressedKeys`, only newly-pressed vs `_prevKeys`; `Back`=backspace, `Enter`=`Confirm`, `Escape`=`Cancel`, else `KeyToChar`→`field.Append`. `KeyToChar` allows lowercase letters/digits/`-` (`:603-610`); `EditorTextField.Sanitize` is the final gate on confirm (`UI/EditorTextField.cs:62-70`). Load mode reads only Escape (`ReadLoadKeyboard`, `:290-296`). Caret is a blinking `"|"` appended using `state.TotalTime % 1.0 &lt; 0.5` (`:419-421`).

**COMPLETE `DispatchNamedAction` op grammar** (the sole registration site, `Composition/EditorOverlay.cs:817-1004`; routed from the headless op channel at `:277-278`):
- `panel:` → `systems` | `scene` | `inspector` | `group &lt;name&gt;` | `inspect &lt;type&gt;` | `select &lt;name&gt;` (`:830-857`)
- `dialog:` → `save-open` | `load-open` | `name &lt;text&gt;` | `confirm` | `cancel` | `cd &lt;folder&gt;` | `up` | `pick &lt;file&gt;` | `load &lt;file&gt;` (`:859-884`)
- `boundary:` → `begin` | `commit` | `cancel` (`:886-899`)
- `trigger:&lt;prefix&gt;` (`:901-910`)
- `ghost:` → `cw` | `ccw` (`:912-927`)
- `asset-band:&lt;entryId&gt;:&lt;band&gt;` (`auto`/`none` clears) (`:929-945`)
- `palette:&lt;id&gt;` | `palette:none` | bare `palette:` (disarm) (`:947-960`)
- `band:&lt;name&gt;` (`:962-969`)
- `order:` → `forward` | `back` (`:971-981`)
- `collider:` → `addBox` | `addConvex` | `remove` | `addVertex` | `deleteVertex` (`:983-998`)
- otherwise `Enum.TryParse&lt;EditorToolbarAction&gt;` → `DispatchToolbarAction` (`:1000-1003`)

**`DispatchToolbarAction` enum handlers** (`:510-568`): `PlayPause`, `Restart`, `ToolMove`, `ToolRotate`, `ToolScale`, `ToggleSnap`, `Save` (opens the dialog, guarded), `Load` (opens the dialog), `Undo`, `Redo`, `OrderForward`, `OrderBack`, `ColliderAddBox`, `ColliderAddConvex`, `ColliderRemove`, `VertexAdd`, `ToolBoundary`, `RefreshCatalog`. The enum itself: `Component/EditorToolbarAction.cs:13-84` (18 actions; `DefaultButtons` in `EditorChromeBuilder.cs:120-142` binds all 18).

---

## 8. VIEWPORT (`ViewportManager`)

**Inset vs chrome rects.** `SetViewportInset(l,t,r,b)` stores four margins (`../rendering/Renderer/ViewportManager.cs:137-149`; negative throws). `Recalculate` (`:204-276`) computes the available area = window minus inset (`:211-214`), then aspect-fits the game viewport inside that centered sub-rect (`:216-243`); `DestinationRectangle` and `ScaleMouseToVirtualCoordinates` both derive from the same `_currentViewport`, so compositing and mouse mapping cannot disagree. All-zero inset == historical full-window letterbox, byte-identical (`:210`).

**Current chrome regions (`UI/EditorChromeLayout.cs`, logical pt × DPR):**
- Top bar: full width × `TopBarHeight 44` (`:29,69-70`).
- Right panel: docked right, width `RightPanelWidth 280`, spanning `TopBarHeight`..`ScreenHeight-BottomBarHeight` (`:31,72-77`).
- Bottom bar: full width × `BottomBarHeight 168`, docked bottom (`:41,79-81`).
- Left: `LeftPanelWidth 0` (`:44`).
- `ViewportInset(scale)` = `(Px(0), Px(44), Px(280), Px(168))` (`:65-66`).

**DevicePixelRatio handling.** `ViewportManager.DevicePixelRatio` (default 1, `:121`); when &gt;1, `ScreenWidth/Height` are device px, `CursorInputSystem` scales the mouse by it (`../cursor/System/CursorInputSystem.cs:60`), and chrome multiplies point metrics via `EditorChromeLayout.Px(points, scale)` (`:60`). `EditorShellSystem` passes `scale = _viewportManager.DevicePixelRatio` (`System/EditorShellSystem.cs:74`). `Composition/EditorHiDpi.cs` sets DevicePixelRatio on macOS Retina under the run flag.

**Window-resize recompute.** `EditorShellSystem.Update` relayouts chrome when `ScreenWidth`/`ScreenHeight`/`scale` differ from the last laid-out values (`System/EditorShellSystem.cs:75-78`) and re-applies `SetViewportInset` (`:80-81`, no-op when unchanged). `EditorChromeRenderSystem.EnsureTargetMatchesWindow` recreates the native target on size change (`:73-82`). `ViewportManager.ScreenWidth/Height` setters `MarkDirty` (`:100,107`); the host feeds them on resize (the `ClientSizeChanged` hook is commented out at `:89` — wired by the head). The right strip and palette also read their region rect every frame / on size change (`EditorPanelSystem.cs:131`; `PalettePlacementSystem.cs:491-495`).

---

## 9. ANIMATION

**No dedicated Easing/Tween utility class exists.** The engine uses MonoGame's `MathHelper.Lerp`/`Clamp`/`SmoothStep` and `Color.Lerp` directly. The canonical frame-rate-based ease is `../ui/ButtonVisualSystem.cs:74-78`:
`current = MathHelper.Lerp(current, target, MathHelper.Clamp(animSpeed * state.Time, 0f, 1f))` with `animSpeed = 18f` default (`:27`) — a ~exponential approach independent of framerate. Camera-follow damping is the other lerp-based ease (`../camera/Component/CameraFollowTargetComponent.cs`). `Transform/OverlayMeshClip.cs` uses `Color.Lerp`/`MathHelper.Lerp` for geometry (not time).

**Delta time.** `GameState.Time` = current-frame `ElapsedGameTime` seconds (`../foundation/State/GameState.cs:28`); `GameState.TotalTime` = total seconds (`:30`). Every `ISystem.Update(GameState state)` has both. Already used: dialog caret blink (`TotalTime`, `EditorDialogSystem.cs:419`); button pop ease (`Time`, `ButtonVisualSystem`).

**How a per-widget hover fade would be ticked.** The three chrome systems (`ToolbarSystem`, `EditorPanelSystem`, `PalettePlacementSystem`) all receive `GameState` in `Update`, so `state.Time` is in hand — but today they do **instant** color writes (no per-widget animation state). A ~120ms fade needs a per-widget progress (0..1) stored somewhere (a new field on `ToolbarButtonComponent`, or a dict keyed by row-index/widget) advanced toward its target with the `ButtonVisualSystem` recipe, inside each chrome system's own `Update`. Do **not** expect `ButtonVisualSystem` to help: it only runs on entities with **both** `SimpleButtonComponent` **and** `ButtonStateComponent` (`../ui/ButtonVisualSystem.cs:26`), and chrome buttons have no `ButtonStateComponent`. The unused `SimpleButtonComponent.VisualScale` "pop" hook (`../ui/SimpleButtonComponent.cs:29`, honored by `ButtonMeshPrepSystem.cs:35-37`) is available if you want a scale pop. Since the button mesh/label is re-prepped from `FillColor` every frame anyway (`ButtonMeshPrepSystem`), per-frame color tweening is cheap.

---

## 10. CONSTRAINTS that bear on regions/tabs

- **`Editor` target rules** (rendering premise): native pixels, `RenderLayer.Native` 1:1, never virtual, never aspect-fit; chrome entities **must not** carry `VisibleComponent` (`EditorChromeBuilder.cs:214-218`); mesh fills **must be opaque**. Any new region/tab widget inherits all three.
- **One shared `Editor` target, one depth stack.** `MasterRenderSystem` sorts *all* entities on the target by `LayerDepth` (`../rendering/System/Draw/MasterRenderSystem.cs:147-153`). The chrome bands: overlays low (0.02–0.04) &lt; panels 0.1 &lt; buttons 0.5 &lt; checkbox-mark 0.55 &lt; thumbnail 0.56 &lt; chip 0.58 &lt; labels 0.6 &lt; dialog 0.70–0.86. New regions/tabs must slot into this stack or they z-fight/occlude.
- **`EditorInfrastructureComponent` restart boundary** (`Component/EditorInfrastructureComponent.cs`; `Composition/EditorTransport.cs:133-168`): every chrome entity **must** set this tag or `Restart` disposes it (all current sites do: `EditorChromeBuilder.cs:226,242,262`; `EditorPanelSystem.cs:498,536,557`; `EditorDialogSystem.cs:511,530`; `PalettePlacementSystem.cs:825,845,869,912`). A new chrome-creation site that forgets it is silently swept on Restart.
- **`GatedSystem` / pipeline ordering** (`Composition/EditorPipelineRegistrar.cs`; weave at `LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:478-673`, documented at `EditorOverlay.cs:44-67`): chrome systems are `RunNormally` and live in both transport states; several editing systems early-out in Play internally. Ordering is load-bearing — `editor.dialog` **early** (before mouse consumers) for modal capture; `editor.selection`/`editor.overlayPrep` after `drawPrep` (final depth); `editor.renderChrome` after game render passes; `finalDraw` last. A new region that hit-tests the cursor must be woven so the dialog's consume still precedes it.
- **Systems-panel toggling of any entry** (`SetEnabled`): if a new region becomes its own pipeline entry, it can be disabled from the panel; the panel guards only its own entry (`EditorPanelSystem.cs:264-280`) — a new interactive region should carry the same self-protection or a user can disable the path back.
- **Culling interactions:** chrome is on `Editor` (never culled). Exceptions: the palette **ghost** is a Main-target sprite whose `VisibleComponent` `CullingSystem` owns (`PalettePlacementSystem.cs:761-764`); thumbnails are `Editor`-target sprites (no cull).
- **No clipping** (see §2): scrollable regions must park whole rows; partial reveal isn't expressible cheaply.

---

## RISKS / SURPRISES

1. **No scissor/clipping anywhere** (`MasterRenderSystem.cs:114-135` all `CullNone`; `GraphicsDevice.Viewport` unset `ViewportManager.cs:273-275`; `../ui/ScrollViewComponent.cs:10` is only a comment). Blender-style panes with partial-row reveal, sub-panel scroll gutters, or splitter-clipped content are **not free**. The CPU clipper (`Transform/OverlayMeshClip.cs`) clips **mesh only** — not text or sprites. Plan for row-granular culling in v1, or budget a real scissor change to the game-agnostic `MasterRenderSystem` (touches every pass), or the HUD-virtual `Scroll` target (one region/screen, wrong space for native chrome).
2. **Stale comment contradicts the transport model.** `LoadLevelExampleGameScreen.cs:669-671` calls the chrome pass "Edit-only internally: outside Edit it renders nothing," but `EditorChromeRenderSystem.Update` checks only `IsEnabled`, **not `RunMode`** (`System/EditorChromeRenderSystem.cs:60-71`), and the premise says chrome renders in **both** Play and Edit while composed. Anyone who assumes "chrome only in Edit" will design wrong — the shell is up during Play too.
3. **No central palette/theme type; colors are duplicated across ≥3 sites.** `EditorChromeBuilder` static fields (`:53-77`) are the de-facto palette, but `EditorDialogSystem.cs:76-77` and `PalettePlacementSystem.cs:84-87` define their own colors, and `EditorPanelSystem.RowColor` hardcodes `Color.White` (`:462-475`). A "strict color palette" refactor must consolidate all of these; `../ui/ButtonTheme.cs` is **not** used by chrome.
4. **Hover is instant and re-derived per frame** by each chrome system writing `FillColor`/text color directly — there is **no per-widget animation state** anywhere. A 120ms fade needs new state + a tick in each chrome system's `Update` (they have `state.Time`); `ButtonVisualSystem` will not help (chrome has no `ButtonStateComponent`).
5. **Modal consume is fragile-by-ordering and load-bearing.** `EditorDialogSystem` clears *all* cursor edges+levels every open frame (`:335-339`) and depends on (a) being woven before every other mouse consumer, (b) the EF1 previous-hardware-state fix (`CursorInputSystem.cs:65-93`), and (c) a single cursor entity. Reordering the pipeline, adding a mouse consumer before the dialog, or a second modal can silently break click-swallowing.
6. **Each chrome system computes its region rect independently** from `EditorChromeLayout` (`EditorPanelSystem.cs:131` `RightPanel`; `PalettePlacementSystem.cs:488` `BottomBar`; `EditorDialogSystem` centers on full screen; toolbar uses `Bounds` baked by `EditorChromeBuilder`). There is **no shared region model** — regions/tabs/splitters mean editing `EditorChromeLayout` **and** every consumer, kept consistent with the single `ViewportInset` 4-tuple.
7. **`ViewportInset` is a single outer (l,t,r,b) rect** — no left region (`LeftPanelWidth 0` hardcoded), no nested panes, no floating panels. The game viewport can only be one centered rect, so a panel that *floats over* the viewport is not expressible via the inset (it would overlap the game). A resizable-splitter shell needs the inset to stay a simple outer margin while chrome subdivides internally.
8. **DPR is pervasive and easy to forget.** Every metric goes through `EditorChromeLayout.Px(points, scale)`; a new region/tab metric that skips it renders half-size on Retina and its hit-test desyncs.
9. **Whole-line scroll is already dictated by the no-clip constraint** (`SystemsPanelLayout.cs:12-15` — a partial line "would bleed over the top/bottom bars, which share the Editor target"). Tabs with internal scroll inherit this.
10. **Right-strip visuals are pooled and re-purposed per frame** (`EditorPanelSystem.cs:378-392`) — any per-widget hover-anim state must key by **row index/content, not entity**, or it smears across scroll. (The palette keeps one entity per item, `:143`, parking scrolled-out ones — a different model, so a shared animation scheme must handle both.)
11. **Nine-patch is effectively unavailable on chrome.** `MasterRenderSystem` treats `NinePatch` as a no-op (`:331-333`); nine-patches are expanded by `SpritePrepSystem`, which runs off the **Main** target. Rounded panels/tabs need a mesh generator (`RoundedRectangleOutline`/`FilledRoundedRectangle`, per rendering premise), not a nine-patch texture.
12. **The toolbar is a flat, non-wrapping row of 18 buttons** (`EditorChromeBuilder.DefaultButtons` `:120-142`, laid out by `EditorChromeLayout.ButtonRow` `:92-105` with no wrap/scroll/overflow) — on a narrow window it runs off the right edge. Tabbed/grouped toolbar chrome is part of the motivation, but there is no overflow handling to build on today.</result>

- **Pure decomposition for the gate** (no test builds a full `EditorOverlay` — it needs a GraphicsDevice): the dirty decision is `SceneCatalog.DecideSwitch` (truth-table tested), confirm-routing is tested on `EditorDialogSystem` directly, and row-click→callback on `EditorPanelSystem`. `EditorOverlay.SelectScene` composes them.
- **Examples `SwitchScene`** sets `RequestedLevelComponent` **only for the host (Game)** screen (bound screens load their own scene via the optional-load); it removes-then-adds so the menu's existing non-remove-first `AddService` can never double-register.
- **Catalog scan gated to the Project tab** — `BuildCatalog`/`ListSceneIds` do desktop directory IO, so they only run when the Project tab is active (not every frame on Scene/Systems).

### Deviations from the design doc (with reasons)
1. **Demos `SwitchScene`/`BindSceneCatalog` not wired** (item 7 says "Demos wires plain LoadScreen"). Demos screens declare no bound scene and have no project context, so their catalog is provably always empty (`SceneCatalogTests.AllPlainScreens_YieldEmptyCatalog`) — the callback would be dead code. Wiring it means editing 5 screens across `MonoDreams.Demos` + four `MonoDreams.Demo.*` projects for zero effect. Demos screens ARE registered with plain display-name `ScreenInfo` (item 2), and their Project tab correctly shows "(no scenes)". The switch seam is fully built + tested via Examples.
2. **Empty-save guard follows item 8, not design §3.5.** Item 8 (authoritative) says refuse iff zero roots + never-loaded, "regardless of whether the target file exists"; §3.5 additionally mentioned the target-file condition. Implemented + truth-table-tested per item 8.
3. **`WebGame.cs` updated** for `ScreenInfo` consistency though the web head is excluded from the gate (couldn't be built without wasm-tools) — mechanical, mirrors Desktop, uses the same public consts, old overload as fallback.
4. **One small shared helper file** (`EditorSceneSwitch.cs`) instead of triplicating the 3 Examples screens' identical hand-off.

---

Congratulations, these are well thought and carefully implemented features, keep this level of attention to details. I've tested it and it works as specified.

Now we can move to UX-E and more improvements and changes I spotted:
 1. The Scene, Systems & Project panels should be moved to the right side of the screen. The right side should still exist and it should hold a dedicated panel for Inspector (it leaves the Scene panel).
 2. Rename "Scene" panel to "Entities", and "Project" to "Scenes".
 3. The central game view (I'll call it Scene panel) should have a Toggle Button/Tab: [Scene mode | Game mode]. In Game mode, just like in Unity's Game view, you can make edits to the game while the game is paused just to test things out, but this doesn't affect the actual scene and these changes aren't to be tracked to later be saved. In Scene mode you edit the actual scene, if you hit "Play" you automatically enter Game mode. (The Play button turns into a Pause button when the game is playing and vice-versa, not needing a separate button for Pause)
 4. Tools should use icons instead of pure text, text should be displayed when the mouse is hovering the tool as a tooltip. You can either render icons as meshes or use Lucide Icons which are free to use, think about what to choose and go for it.
 5. Tools like bringing an entity forward or back should be available under a context menu: right clicking in the Scene panel should bring up a menu about the current selected object, a submenu should be "Order" and it should house the actions to Bring forward and to Send backwards. This menu should also be available as a fixed top menu named "Entity". The context menu (right click menu) on the Entities panel, should enable one to add an Empty entity, for example. Or under de Scenes panel, a "Create empty scene".
 6. These tools should be at the top of the panel, not the window, follow Blender's UI philosophy and UX of each panel having its own "life", with its own top bar, with tools, tabs, menus, it's own context menu for right clicking. This way everything is mode modular.
 7. The game camera should be an Entity and the camera should be visible when you're not in camera view (dragging the view away from the default camera view or chaning zoom) just like in Blender 3D UI, the camera may be represented by its boundaries and having crossing lines connecting the opposite edges of the boundaries, forming an "x", just like in Blender. There should be a tool/button to quickly go back to camera view, just like we have in the Blender's 3D editor top right corner quick buttons for navigation and view.
 8. When placing assets the asset whould be centered in the mouse location. Also, scaling an asset is currently broken, it seems to scale its boundaries but not the asset/sprite itself.

## Audit result (pre-mortem #1 — does any path pre-bake `WorldScale` into `DrawComponent.Size`?)
**No.** Every `DrawComponent.Size` writer sets a source/destination pixel size independent of the transform scale, so composing multiplies `WorldScale` in exactly once:
- `SpritePrepSystem` — `Size = SpriteInfoComponent.Size` (source), `Scale = Transform.WorldScale` (separate fields).
- `SpritePropFactory` — writes `SpriteInfoComponent.Size = source dims` (not `DrawComponent`).
- Palette ghost — `SpriteInfoComponent.Size = source dims` → `SpritePrepSystem` fills the `DrawComponent` (ghost transform scale 1).
- Palette thumbnail (Editor target, bypasses `SpritePrepSystem`) — sets `Size` to the fitted box, leaves `Scale` at default `One` → fix is a no-op (asserted).
- Textured cursor (`Cursor.Create`) — `Size=(32,32)`, `Scale` default `One` → byte-identical. Mesh cursor uses the Mesh type (unaffected).
- `SceneReaderSystem.RestoreDrawComponents` — bare `DrawComponent` (`Size` unset) → `SpritePrepSystem` fills it.

`DrawComponent.Scale` defaults to `Vector2.One` and only `SpritePrepSystem` ever writes a non-unit value, so the fix is byte-identical to the old `Size/source` for every unscaled sprite; only a world-scaled sprite changes (the bug). Text and no-source-rect (nine-patch) paths untouched.

## Fixes
- **Bug 1 (rendering):** `MasterRenderSystem` — extracted `internal static ComputeSpriteScale(DrawComponent)` = `(Size/source)·Scale` in the source-rect branch (was `Size/source`, discarding `Scale`). This matches `GizmoTransform.SpriteWorldQuad`'s `WorldScale·(Size/source)` product, so the drawn quad equals the pick/outline quad.
- **Bug 2 (level-editor):** `PalettePlacementSystem` — new shared `SpritePlacementPosition` (used by both ghost and every stamp) offsets the transform position by the source-space centre↔origin delta (rotated by armed rotation) so the sprite's **visual centre** lands at the cursor; `Origin` stays feet-anchored. **Decision:** snap quantizes the **transform (feet/origin) position** — the same field it quantized before (documented in the premise). Triggers keep placing at the raw snapped cursor. Multi-stamp arc-length spacing is measured on the raw cursor path (unaffected).

## Tests &amp; premises
- New `MonoDreams.Tests/Rendering/SpriteDrawScaleTests.cs` (4 tests): world-scaled quad matches `SpriteWorldQuad`; unit scale byte-identical to old `Size/source`; deliberate-`Size`/unit-`Scale` (thumbnail/cursor) does not double-scale; no-source-rect uses raw `Scale`.
- Updated 4 shifted assertions in `PalettePlacementTests` (ghost follow+snap, single-click, multi-stamp).
- Rendering premise: added "A sprite's drawn quad honors `Transform.WorldScale` exactly once" (with the audit). Level-editor premise: updated feet-origin/palette anchor math.

- `MonoDreams.Cli.Tests`: **31 passed / 0 failed**.
- `MonoDreams.Tests`: **528 passed** (one integration test, `InfiniteRunnerTests.PlayerCollectsCharm`, flaked under the parallel full run and **passed on isolated retry** — it's Examples runner collision logic, unaffected by a render or palette change since Examples-headless early-returns from `Draw`).

## Summary

Implemented the UX2-C icon toolbar + tooltips + tool relocation for the MonoDreams editor, on branch `feat/level-editor`.

### What was done

**New files**
- `MonoDreams/level-editor/UI/EditorIcons.cs` — pure geometry library. Each glyph (Move/Rotate/Scale/Boundary/Snap, Play/Pause/Restart, Save/Undo/Redo/Refresh) is a line/triangle primitive list authored in a unit box and instantiated into a pixel rect via a private `Pen` helper — the `ArrowTriangle` disclosure-caret pattern generalized. Lucide is the visual reference only (nothing imported). `ForAction`/`Resolve`/`HasIcon` map actions→icons (PlayPause swaps by transport state); `CenteredIconRect` places the square glyph. Undo/Redo and Restart/Refresh are exact horizontal mirrors (`u → 1-u`); all geometry scales purely with the rect.
- `MonoDreams/level-editor/UI/EditorTooltip.cs` — pure delay gate + cursor-anchored, window-clamped placement + box sizing.
- `MonoDreams/level-editor/System/EditorTooltipSystem.cs` — ONE pooled tooltip (Bg2 box + Border outline mesh + Text0 label), parked when idle, shown after ~0.45s hover, hidden instantly on move-off/press.
- Tests: `EditorIconsTests.cs`, `EditorTooltipTests.cs`.

**Key decisions**
- **Icon-mesh representation**: icon buttons carry a separate screen-baked mesh entity (`ToolbarButtonComponent.IconEntity`) — identity `WorldMatrix`, Editor target, `EditorInfrastructureComponent`, no `VisibleComponent`/`SimpleButtonComponent` — refilled every frame by `ToolbarSystem.BakeIcon` (exactly the disclosure-arrow pattern). The button *body* keeps its `SimpleButtonComponent` fill/outline; only the label was replaced. Icon color: `TextDisabled` inert → `Success` for Snap-on → `Accent` for the active radio tool (via new `EditorToolbarAction.IsActiveIn` over the shared `GizmoStateComponent`) → `Color.Lerp(Text1, Text0, hover)` idle→hover.
- **Tooltip state home**: per-button `HoverSeconds` clock advanced (and reset on move-off/press) by `ToolbarSystem`; `EditorTooltipSystem` reads it to pick the winner. Simplest correct option — `ToolbarSystem` already iterates buttons and owns the cursor. New `Depths.Tooltip`/`TooltipLabel` sit above `DialogLabel`.
- **Actions with no icon** (Order/collider/vertex) stay text buttons until UX2-D — `EditorChromeBuilder` builds icon-vs-text per `EditorIcons.HasIcon`.
- **Relocation**: the 5 tools moved from `DefaultButtons` into `HeaderButtons` after the transport; `ButtonRowIn` gained a `separatorAfterIndex` + `ClusterGap` for the transport·gap·tools clustering.

**Modified**: `ToolbarButtonComponent` (IconEntity/Tooltip/HoverSeconds), `EditorToolbarAction` (IsActiveIn), `EditorTheme` (Depths.Tooltip/TooltipLabel), `EditorChromeLayout` (ClusterGap + separator), `EditorChromeBuilder`, `ToolbarSystem`, `EditorOverlay` (constructs+exposes `Tooltip`), the 4 editor-composed screens (weave `tooltip` into `editor.toolbar`), `ToolbarTests`/`EditorTransportTests`, and the premises (new icon/tooltip premise + toolbar-premise relocation note + disclosure-arrow cross-reference; no `Tests: none yet`).

## Summary

Implemented the editor context-menu wave (`editor-shell-ui-ux-2.md` §4) end-to-end. Pushed `41e852a..a01a405` to `origin/feat/level-editor` across 5 commits:
- `336ba24` primitive · `b762344` wiring · `5b3696b` tests · `61ca91f` docs · `a01a405` pressed-fill polish

### The `EditorContextMenu` primitive (menu-model shape)
- **`MonoDreams/level-editor/UI/EditorContextMenuModel.cs`** — pure DATA: `EditorMenuItem { Kind (Action/Separator/Submenu), Label, Path, Enabled, Danger, Submenu }` + builders `EntityMenu` / `EntitiesPanelMenu` / `ScenesPanelMenu` + `FindByPath`. Action-id paths (`order/forward`, `order/back`, `delete`, `add-empty`, `create-scene`) are the stable ids the overlay maps and `menu:pick` matches. **One model renders two ways** (cursor context menu via `OpenAt`, header dropdown via `OpenBelow`).
- **`.../UI/EditorContextMenuLayout.cs`** — pure pixel math: window-clamped box, item/submenu rects (submenu opens right, flips left when no room), ▸ caret, separator line.
- **`.../System/EditorContextMenuSystem.cs`** — `OpenAt`/`OpenBelow`/`Pick`/`Close`/`IsOpen`; keyboard-Escape, hover+submenu, click-away, item dispatch; modal cursor-consume (the dialog recipe); `isBlocked` refuses to open while the dialog is open or a shell drag owns the pointer. New `EditorTheme.Depths.Menu*` band **above the tooltip** (documented — a menu and dialog are never open together).

### Right-click composition (the load-bearing decision)
Right-click **detection lives where the context is already resolved**, not in the menu primitive:
- **Viewport** → `SelectionSystem`: on a right-press in `SelectTransform` (dormant when a tool is armed, so palette/boundary keep right-click-as-disarm; skipped when the gizmo claimed the press), it reuses the **same `TryPick`** as left-click selection (never forked), selects the hit via the new `SelectExclusive`, and raises `ViewportContextMenuRequested`. Empty viewport opens nothing and clears nothing. Right-button only — gizmo `PressClaimed` and middle-drag camera-nav are untouched.
- **Panels** → `EditorPanelSystem`: a right-press in the left strip raises `ContextMenuRequested` and consumes the press (so the palette can't disarm); `EntityAtPoint` maps the row.
- **`EditorOverlay.OpenContextMenu(EditorMenuContext, state)`** is the ONE coordinator for all four surfaces + the `menu:open`/`menu:pick`/`menu:close` ops; the `Entity ▾` header button (`EditorToolbarAction.EntityMenu` text button + a font-independent ▾ caret mesh) dispatches into it. `DispatchMenuAction` maps paths to the shared instances (Order → within-band nudges, Delete → snapshotting delete, Add Empty Entity → `EditorCommandSystem.AddEmptyEntity` undoable create at `Camera.Position`, Create Empty Scene → the dialog).

### Create Empty Scene + Order relocation
- New `EditorDialogSystem.CreateScene` mode (injected collision predicate → loud refuse + stay open; injected create callback → the overlay writes an empty world via `SceneWriter`/`CanonicalJson`, `EnsureLevelBundled(id)`, then dirty-gated `SelectScene`). Keeps the dialog game-agnostic. `dialog:name|confirm|cancel` drive it.
- `OrderForward`/`OrderBack` **buttons removed** from the window bar (actions + dispatch stay); collider/vertex text buttons remain.
- Menu woven `editor.contextMenu` right after `editor.dialog` on **every** overlay screen (3 Examples + 5 Demos/module demos); `ShouldSuppressInput` now ORs `Menu.IsOpen`.

### Verify gate (full `dotnet test --configuration Release`, repo root)
- **MonoDreams.Tests: 580 passed**, 1 fail = `InfiniteRunnerTests.PlayerCollectsCharm` (process-exit timeout — the documented flake; **passes 3/3 on isolated retry**, and my InfiniteRunner edit is inside `if (_editor != null)`, inert on that non-editor gameplay replay).
- **MonoDreams.Cli.Tests: 31 passed.**
- New coverage: `EditorContextMenuTests` (24 — model/layout/modality/wiring/AddEmpty/CreateScene), viewport right-click in `SelectionTests`, panel right-click in `EditorPanelTests`, Order-relocation updates in `ToolbarTests`.

### Deviations
- **Op verb `menu:open entity`** (not the old design's `entity-menu`) — matches the task scope; design §4 updated.
- **No `RightDown`/`RightUp` op added** to the headless channel (would've been extra scope). The interactive right-click is covered by system-level tests; the headless flow uses `menu:open`/`menu:pick`. Recorded in design §4.
- The overlay's op-parsing + `OpenContextMenu` coordinator + `CreateEmptyScene` write/bundle/switch orchestration aren't unit-tested (the overlay is GraphicsDevice-bound — consistent with the existing untested `DispatchToolbarAction`/`DispatchNamedAction`/`SaveCurrentScene`); the constituent pieces are unit-tested and the composed menu is exercised by the `UniversalOverlayTests`/`DemosEditorOverlayTests` integration suite.
