## What was done

Split the single `Camera` into the **free editor VIEW** (unchanged — `CameraNavSystem` still drives it) and a standalone **camera rig** holding the authored game-camera state, exactly per design §6.

**New code** (`MonoDreams/level-editor/`): `Component/CameraRigComponent.cs` (zoom+rotation; position lives on its `TransformComponent`), `Transform/CameraRigGlyph.cs` (pure frustum-rect + view≈rig epsilon math), `Composition/EditorCameraRig.cs` (owns the rig entity + glyph emit + `SnapViewToRig` + `AsCamera` for Save + `SyncFromScene`).

**Modified**: `SceneReaderSystem` (rig seam + camera split), `SelectionSystem` (rig border-pick), `GizmoSystem` (force Move for the rig), `EditorCommandSystem` (delete-guard), `EditorOverlayPrepSystem` (glyph emit), `EditorToolbarAction`+`EditorIcons`+`EditorTheme`+`EditorChromeLayout`+`EditorChromeBuilder` (the `CameraView` nav button, right-corner-anchored, + Camera frustum icon + `CameraGlyph` role/depth), `EditorOverlay` (wires it all: rig construction, reader seam, prep pass, the 3 `BuildScene` calls now read the rig, `view:camera` op).

## Key decisions (as requested)

- **Reader camera split** — audit finding: the reader **never applied `scene.camera` today**; it only auto-framed on content, and every existing scene saves `camera: null`. So the split is a strict superset: **editor present** (new `applyCameraToRig` seam wired) → `scene.camera` to the rig + the VIEW auto-frames content; **shipped** (no seam) → `scene.camera` applied to the live camera when present, else auto-frame (byte-identical for the null-camera scenes all prior tests use). The reference shipped screen (`LoadLevelExampleGameScreen`) is **untouched** (passes the reader no camera → `CameraFollowSystem` owns it, as before).
- **Rig picking** — the rig is a **first-class entity** (its own `TransformComponent` is the data), **not** a `ProxyBindingKind`. Folded into `SelectionSystem` as a sibling **border-pick on the frustum world-rect at `ProxyBorderPickDepth`** (same ordering as collider proxies) and moved by the standard `TransformEditCommand` (gizmo forced to Move like a proxy). The proxy seam is for component-local data that *isn't* an entity; the rig *is* one — documented in the proxy premise.
- **Epsilon** — `CameraRigGlyph.PositionEpsilon = 0.5` world units, `ZoomEpsilon = 1e-3` (documented in the class + premise).
- **Save** — `SceneWriter.BuildScene` signature **unchanged**; the overlay passes `EditorCameraRig.AsCamera()` (a throwaway `Camera` from the rig). Narrowest change — every byte-stability/round-trip test call site compiles unchanged. `.mdscene` format untouched.

## Deviation

`GizmoSystem.BuildOutline` for the rig keeps the existing 16px-box fallback rather than tracing the frustum rect — the glyph already draws the frustum when the view differs, and `BuildOutline` is static (no camera access); the move handle + glyph are the affordances. Minor visual, no functional impact.

<result>UX2-F is complete, green, and pushed. Here is the final report.

## What was built (wave UX2-F — Scene/Game mode sandbox)

The editor now has the user-confirmed Unity-style snapshot model. `EditorTransport` is the ONE owner of both `RunMode` and a new `EditorViewMode { Scene, Game }` (default Scene). Entering Game mode snapshots the scene in memory and looks through the game camera; sandbox edits are discarded on exit by restoring the snapshot through the reader.

## Key decisions (the two you flagged)

- **In-memory restore entry point** — a `LoadSceneRequest(SceneData)` constructor overload (not a public reader method). The reader's `On()` branches on `message.Scene` and hands off to a shared `Load(scene, …)` that the file path also calls. Chosen so **every** load still flows through the ONE message + subscriber, keeping the message-driven premise coherent and the reader the single restore implementation (pre-mortem #2). The overlay's `RestoreSnapshot` seam is just `world.Publish(new LoadSceneRequest(snapshot))`, so re-tag, texture rehydration (content + `file:` keys), `DrawComponent` restore, and camera-rig re-sync are all shared.
- **Snapshot timing** — `EditorTransport.Play` calls `EnterGameMode` (which takes the snapshot) **before** `state.RunMode = Play`, so no simulation frame can corrupt the restore point (pre-mortem #7). An order test asserts `RunMode == Edit` at capture time.

## Other notable decisions / deviations

- **The `[Scene | Game]` toggle is two `ToolbarButtonComponent` segments** the ONE `ToolbarSystem` hit-tests + dispatches (following the UX2-E camera-view-button precedent), rendered **tab-style** by a new `RenderSegment` branch reading a `Func&lt;EditorViewMode&gt;` seam + a new `ToolbarButtonComponent.UnderlineEntity`; `IsModeToggle` joins `IsTransport` in the both-modes gate. This avoided a new pipeline system and any screen-weaving churn across the 4–6 screen composition sites — consistent with the "ONE ToolbarSystem" premise. Geometry lives in one place (`EditorChromeLayout.ModeToggleSegments`/`ModeToggleReservedWidth`); the transport row offsets right by the reserved width.
- **`SaveBlockReason.GameMode`** slots between `Playing` and `NoProjectRoot`; `SaveBlock` gained an optional `viewMode` param (defaults `Scene`, so pre-UX2-F callers are byte-identical). The toolbar Save-dim predicate and all three save paths (dispatch, `SaveCurrentSceneTo`, `SaveBackupAs`) refuse through it. The dialog/ops share the same guard by construction; I unit-tested the pure guard + the toolbar refusal (the overlay/dialog are GraphicsDevice-bound, not unit-constructable).
- **`EditorHistory.MarkDirty()`** restores a captured dirty state after `Clear()` without adding an undo entry (so undo-after-exit stays a no-op).

## Summary

**Part 1 — blank/truncated chrome text bug (framework fix).** `TextPrepSystem` (`MonoDreams/rendering-text/System/Draw/TextPrepSystem.cs`) now scopes the reveal gate to revealing text: the `VisibleCharacterCount` slice + early-out apply **only when `RevealingSpeed &gt; 0`**; static (non-revealing) text renders its full `TextContent` regardless of the count, and only genuinely empty/null content renders nothing. The load-bearing decision is extracted into a pure, font-free `TextPrepSystem.TryGetVisibleText(...)` (testable without a GraphicsDevice — matching the `GizmoTransform`/`CameraNav` pattern). Dialogue reveal is untouched (`RevealingSpeed &gt; 0` still count-gates).

**Part 2 — camera rig discoverability.**
- **Tree row:** `EditorPanelSystem` folds the rig back into the Entities tree (a unioned `With&lt;CameraRigComponent&gt;` set in `MaterializeScene`) as the ONE explicit infra include, labelled **"Camera"** (labeler special-case); clicking it selects the rig two-way (Inspector shows `TransformComponent` + `CameraRigComponent`).
- **Scale → zoom:** the gizmo's new `ResolveTool` makes Move **and** Scale legal for the rig (Rotate stays disabled→Move). Scale edits `CameraRigComponent.Zoom` via a new undoable, drag-coalesced `CameraZoomEditCommand` (bigger frustum ⇒ lower zoom: `newZoom = beforeZoom / GizmoTransform.ScaleFactor`, clamped to `CameraNavSystem.DefaultMinZoom..DefaultMaxZoom` = 0.25–4.0). One drag = one undo step; dirties the scene; glyph + border-pick track the live zoom.
- **Selection ease:** the rig's border-pick already uses the identical `ProxyBorderPickTolerancePixels` (÷zoom) as the collider/boundary proxies — no change needed; noted.

## Hide-by-count audit result
Only **two** genuine non-revealing `VisibleCharacterCount = 0` hides existed, both in `PalettePlacementSystem` (`CreateLabel`, `ParkButton`) — and both **already park the label off-screen**, so I converted them to rely on the off-screen park (set create to `int.MaxValue`, dropped the now-defunct count-0 reset). All dialogue `count = 0` sites are either **revealing** (`RevealingSpeed = 20`, count-gated typewriter — preserved) or **clear `TextContent`** (empty → renders nothing) — unaffected.

Code: `TextPrepSystem.cs`, `PalettePlacementSystem.cs`, `GizmoSystem.cs`, `GizmoTransform.cs`, `EditorPanelSystem.cs`, new `Undo/CameraZoomEditCommand.cs`. Great, now a new wave of improvements and fixes:
 1. Just launching the editor from some project and the first thing you do is switch to Game mode, makes the entire scene disappear, returning to Scene mode doesn't help.
 2. Rename the toggles to be more explicit: "Scene mode" and "Game mode". And switching to Game mode automatically "plays" the game.
 3. [Image #3] The arrow pointers (triangles) can be made a little more proeminent, it's hard to even see them in all symbols of grabbing, rotating, scaling, undo/redo, etc. The Snap to grid symbol looks more like a hashtag than to a grid, draw a square to make the borders closed and it will look more like a grid. Also the camera view icon is too hard to understand, make it more obvious more like this: [Image #5]. The save icon can be improved as well, more like this: [Image #6] (with a bevel on the top right corner and the top rectangle displaced a *little* to the left.
 4. [Image #4] Just like in Blender we want a menu to control viewport overlays, like showing grid lines, adjusting the spacing in the grid, outline selected or not, etc.
 5. Let's have keyboard shortcuts, for now they don't need to be configurable. We'll copy Blender 3D shortcuts: Shift + A to add something, Ctrl/Cmd + Z to undo, Ctrl/Cmd + Shift + Z to redo, G to grab/move, S to scale, R to rotate. Shift + A and others are combo shortcuts, this will need a little hard work on how we process input, this work is not to be super specialized to the editor, meaning any future game features that want to use combo inputs will be able to leverage it. Build also the same support blender gives that once you enter Grab mode you can hit X to move along the X axis, Y for Y axis, you can type in how many units you want it to be moved (those need to be displayed at the viewport editor bottom just like in Blender, this means adding a bottom status bar just like blender and IntelliJ have). These shortcuts are contextual, so these behaviors are only active when the cursor/focus is on the viewport panel.

<result>Pushed (`6ca81be..bc455da`). All work is complete and verified. Here is my final report.

## WHAT THE REPRO PINNED — the "returning doesn't help" half

I wrote the integration repro first and ran it against unmodified code (`GameModeBlankSceneReproTests`). The diagnostic trace pinned it exactly:

```
BOOT:   view=(1283,-522)  rig=(0,0)   visible=True    ← view auto-framed on content; rig stuck at origin
GAME:   view=(0,0)                    visible=False   ← SnapViewToRig → view to origin → content culled
BACK:   view=(1283,-522) alive=True pos=(1275,-530) visible=True  ← Scene view + entities FULLY restored
REGAME: view=(0,0)                    visible=False   ← re-enter Game → STILL blank
```

Returning to Scene mode **does** fully restore the Scene view and the entities. The bug is that it never cures the underlying defect: the camera **rig stays at the origin**. Every existing scene persists `camera: null` (the UX2-E audit), so `SyncFromScene(null)` left the rig at its pre-load ctor default. The Game-mode snapshot's `scene.camera` is then built from that origin rig (`rig.AsCamera()`), so the exit-restore re-syncs the rig to the origin *again* — the origin rig is **self-perpetuating**, so **every** Game-mode entry is blank. "Returning doesn't help" = returning doesn't move the rig onto the content. (It is NOT an unwired-CaptureView or a failed-restore — those were the candidate suspects; the empirical run ruled them out for the wired path. The zeroed-capture path is a real *latent* blank, hardened by fix #2.)

## Fixes (files + decisions)
- **Fix #1 — `SceneReaderSystem.ApplyCamera`**: sequence the editor rig-sync **after** the view auto-frames, and for a `camera: null` scene hand the rig the just-framed view (`scene.Camera ?? CameraDataFromView()`) — "the authored camera starts on the content". A scene that *has* a camera still syncs verbatim.
- **Fix #2 — `EditorTransport`**: added `CameraViewSnapshot.IsValid` (positive zoom); `ExitToSceneMode` applies the captured view only when valid, so a zeroed/unwired capture can't blank the view via `Camera.Zoom`'s 0→0.1 clamp. The overlay already wires `CaptureView`/`RestoreView` unconditionally in its ctor (centralized — no screen can miss it; the only production `new EditorTransport` is there); the guard is defense-in-depth. Chose the guard over a "centralized wiring test" because the overlay ctor needs a GraphicsDevice (not headless-constructible).
- **Fix #3 — exit restores the world**: the repro proves the healthy path end-to-end; the reader's existing fail-loud path (`Logger.Error` + re-throw) covers the failure mode. Deliberately did **not** add a try-catch that would swallow/hide a failed restore.
- **Fix #4 — labels**: segments read **"Scene mode" / "Game mode"**; `ModeSegmentWidth` recomputed 52→92pt (measured "Scene mode" ≈80pt at LabelScale from the real font). `ModeToggleReservedWidth` follows automatically; the DPR test reads the constant so it still passes.
- **Fix #5 — auto-play**: `ModeGame` (toggle segment + `mode:game` op) now dispatches `Transport.Play` (was `EnterGameMode`), reusing the exact Play-in-Scene-mode composition so snapshot-before-RunMode-flip (pre-mortem #7) holds. Play/Pause-in-Game, exit-lands-Paused, and Restart unchanged.

## Tests (all green)

## What changed
Geometry-only edits to `MonoDreams/level-editor/UI/EditorIcons.cs` (all colors still flow through the caller's `EditorTheme` role — no color tokens added), matching premise text in `MonoDreams/level-editor/docs/premises.md`, and new pure-geometry tests in `MonoDreams.Tests/LevelEditor/EditorIconsTests.cs`.

## Per-icon geometry decisions (unit-box numbers)
- **Arrowheads** — new shared `ArrowSize = 0.24` (was ~0.13). Head base = widest span = 1.2×0.24 = **0.288 → 28.8% of the box** (≥22% with ~31% margin), applied to Move (all 4 heads), Rotate (arc head), Scale (both heads), Undo, Redo, Restart, Refresh. Stroke thickness kept at 0.085 — the heads carry the prominence; thickening the 8-line grid would crowd it (the "thicken where needed" clause).
- **Scale** — redesigned to a double-headed diagonal shaft (0.26,0.74)→(0.74,0.26) with a prominent head at each end (↗ at 0.76,0.24 and ↙ at 0.24,0.76), so "both heads" is literal.
- **Snap** — closed square border [0.16,0.84]² (4 strokes) + inner division lines at the thirds (≈0.387, ≈0.613), 2 vertical + 2 horizontal, full-span → **8 strokes, 9 cells** (kills the "#" reading).
- **Camera** — video-camera: body rect [0.10,0.36]–[0.66,0.72] (**width 0.56 = 56% of the box**), a top tab (3 open lines, 0.24–0.42 × 0.26–body-top), and a filled lens triangle with **apex (0.66,0.54) on the body's right edge**, base (0.88,0.42)&amp;(0.88,0.66).
- **Save** — floppy with **beveled top-right**: top stops at 0.62, diagonal bevel (0.62,0.18)→(0.82,0.38), then right/bottom/left; **shutter** FillQuad (0.28,0.18)–(0.54,0.40) centre-x **0.41 (left of centre)**; **label** FillQuad (0.34,0.54)–(0.66,0.78) centre-x **0.50 (centred)**.

Mirror pairs (Undo/Redo, Restart/Refresh) stay exact horizontal reflections by construction (same code + `Mirror` flag), so those assertions hold with the enlarged heads.
